📚 本期文献进展 / Literature Brief

  • 本期季节性流感病毒研究集中呈现以下进展。
  • 在 4694 名老年 ILI 患者中,1587 名(33.81%)流感阳性,其中 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 为主要亚型(93.3%)。疫苗接种 覆盖率为 32.72% (1536/4694)。
  • 蒿提取物显着抑制 H1N1pdm09 和 H3N2 病毒复制,在感染细胞中观察到病毒滴度降低 70%,并显示出对 TLR4/MyD88/NF-κB 信号通路的显着调节。 [10.3390/ph19081170]。
  • 主要结论是,在“同一个健康”框架下加强兽医 监测 可以改善病毒重配事件的检测,增强病毒传播的可追溯性,并支持集约化畜牧系统的及时风险评估。台湾的经验表明,将分子流行病学、兽医现场监测和跨部门沟通相结合,为流行病防范提供了实用价值。
  • 与大流行前相比,大流行后 6 个月 17 岁和 18-64 岁儿童中针对 甲型H3N2流感病毒 和乙型流感的 VE 显着升高。相反,大流行后 18-64 岁人群中 VE 相对于 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 的水平较低。≥65 岁的人群没有观察到显着差异,并且 B/维多利亚数据有限。
  • This reporting window highlights the following research developments.
  • Of the 4694 enrolled elderly patients with ILI, 1587 (33.81%) were influenza-positive, with A(H1N1)pdm09 being the dominant subtype (93.3%). Vaccination coverage was 32.72% (1536/4694). [10.1186/s12877-026-08049-7].
  • Artemisia extracts significantly suppressed H1N1pdm09 and H3N2 viral replication, with a 70% reduction in viral titers observed in infected cells, and demonstrated a notable modulation of the TLR4/MyD88/NF-κB signaling pathway.
  • The key conclusion is that strengthened veterinary surveillance under a One Health framework can improve the detection of viral reassortment events, enhance traceability of viral spread, and support timely risk assessment in intensive livestock systems.
  • Post-pandemic VE against A(H3N2) and influenza B was significantly higher in 6-month-17-year-olds and 18-64-year-olds compared to pre-pandemic.

📰 本期新闻动态 / News Brief

  • 本期季节性流感病毒新闻集中呈现以下动态。
  • 世界卫生组织关于 2027 年南半球流感季节流感病毒 疫苗 组成的磋商。该事件已确认,涉及分析 监测 数据以更新 疫苗 建议。 [news-afb83310cca9d960]。
  • 世卫组织报告了 2026 年第 28 周的全球呼吸道病毒活动,其中流感阳性率低于 10%,全球 SARS-CoV-2 活动仍然较低。乙型流感病毒在检测到的流感病毒中占主导地位。 [news-a6187b2ed473a1eb]。
  • 塔斯马尼亚卫生部报告了急性呼吸道感染活动的官方更新,包括 COVID-19、流感和 RSV。 [news-7bbe2472bd9df167]。
  • 已证实:Moderna 基于 mRNA 的季节性流感 疫苗 在一项后期国际试验中优于传统流感疫苗,其功效比 GSK 批准的标准剂量流感疫苗高 26.6%。 [news-e3a9541b8b0e6a1c]。
  • This reporting window highlights the following Seasonal influenza viruses news developments.
  • WHO Consultation on the composition of influenza virus vaccines for the 2027 southern hemisphere influenza season. The event is confirmed and involves analyzing surveillance data to update vaccine recommendations.
  • WHO reported global respiratory virus activity for week 28 2026, with influenza positivity below 10% and SARS-CoV-2 activity remaining low globally. Influenza B viruses were predominant among influenza detections.
  • Official update on acute respiratory infection activity, including COVID-19, influenza, and RSV, reported by the Tasmanian Department of Health. [news-7bbe2472bd9df167].
  • Confirmed: Moderna's mRNA-based seasonal flu vaccine outperformed conventional flu shots in a late-stage international trial, showing 26.6% higher efficacy than an approved standard-dose flu shot from GSK.

📘 主报告：研究论文 / Primary Research

Journal: Journal of Medical Virology  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-08-01  |  DOI: 10.1002/jmv.71075  |  Date basis: first_publication_date  |  Publication status: Future scheduled publication  |  Citation: 2026, 98, 8, e71075

2025-2026 年冬季巴基斯坦二级护理 监测 站点流感 甲型H3N2流感病毒 病毒的分子 监测 和基因组特征

Molecular Surveillance and Genomic Characterization of Influenza A(H3N2) Viruses From a Secondary‐Care Surveillance Site in Pakistan During the 2025–2026 Winter Season
作者： Aamir Javed, Jamal Hayat, Syed Adnan Haider, Zunera Jamal, Rabia Hakim, Qasim Ali, Mir Muhammad Hassan Bullo, Muhammad Tahir, Muhammad Salman, Yusra Javed
摘要中文翻译摘要 呼吸道病毒感染,特别是甲型流感和 SARS-CoV-2,仍然是巴基斯坦等流行地区的主要公共卫生问题,那里的高流感样疾病负担和有限的基因组数据强调需要 监测 来指导 疫苗 策略。本研究评估了 2025 年至 2026 年冬季伊斯兰堡一家二级护理医院流感样病例中的呼吸道病毒传播和遗传多样性。在 170 例 qRT-PCR 检测病例中,甲型流感检出率为 24.1%,SARS-CoV-2 检出率为 1.17%,未检出乙型流感。女性甲型流感阳性率略高(25.0%),11-20 岁人群的负担最高。对 21 种流感 甲型H3N2流感病毒 分离株的全 基因组 测序显示,亚支 K (52.4%)、J.2.2 (28.6%) 和 J.2 (9.5%) 存在共循环。系统发育分析显示,亚进化枝 K 与来自北美、大洋洲、欧洲和 WHO 推荐的 2026-2027 年北半球 H3N2 疫苗 菌株的最新分离株聚类,而 J.2 和 J.2.2 与早期的 疫苗 样菌株一致。NA 分析显示分支 B.4.2.2 与 K 谱系相关以及与最近的北美菌株相关性的优势。亚进化枝 K 表现出保守的 HA 取代(K2N、S144N、N158D、I160K、Q173R、T328A)和变化有限的 HA2 S49N。未检测到 抗病毒药物 抗性 突变。总体而言,研究结果证明了 H3N2 亚支 K 的优势和遗传稳定性,并支持连续 基因组监测 用于 疫苗 菌株选择和流感准备。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
呼吸道病毒感染,特别是甲型流感和 SARS-CoV-2,仍然是巴基斯坦等流行地区的主要公共卫生问题,那里的高流感样疾病负担和有限的基因组数据强调需要 监测 来指导 疫苗 策略。
设计与对象
本研究评估了 2025 年至 2026 年冬季伊斯兰堡一家二级护理医院流感样病例中的呼吸道病毒传播和遗传多样性。
核心方法
在 170 例 qRT-PCR 检测病例中,甲型流感检出率为 24.1%,SARS-CoV-2 检出率为 1.17%,未检出乙型流感。对 21 种流感 甲型H3N2流感病毒 分离株的全 基因组 测序显示,亚支 K (52.4%)、J.2.2 (28.6%) 和 J.2 (9.5%) 存在共循环。
主要结果
女性甲型流感阳性率略高(25.0%),11-20 岁人群的负担最高。系统发育分析显示,亚进化枝 K 与来自北美、大洋洲、欧洲和 WHO 推荐的 2026-2027 年北半球 H3N2 疫苗 菌株的最新分离株聚类,而 J.2 和 J.2.2 与早期的 疫苗 样菌株一致。NA 分析显示分支 B.4.2.2 与 K 谱系相关以及与最近的北美菌株相关性的优势。亚进化枝 K 表现出保守的 HA 取代(K2N、S144N、N158D、I160K、Q173R、T328A)和变化有限的 HA2 S49N。未检测到 抗病毒药物 抗性 突变。
解释与创新
总体而言,研究结果证明了 H3N2 亚支 K 的优势和遗传稳定性,并支持连续 基因组监测 用于 疫苗 菌株选择和流感准备。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果通过显示 K 分支与 WHO 推荐菌株的一致性来为 疫苗 菌株选择提供信息,并强调 基因组监测 在流感防范中的重要性。
局限与证据强度
该研究是在一家二级保健医院进行的,限制了普遍性。然而,由于全面的基因组分析和直接检测方法,证据强度很高。

Molecular Surveillance and Genomic Characterization of Influenza A(H3N2) Viruses From a Secondary‐Care Surveillance Site in Pakistan During the 2025–2026 Winter Season

Authors: Aamir Javed, Jamal Hayat, Syed Adnan Haider, Zunera Jamal, Rabia Hakim, Qasim Ali, Mir Muhammad Hassan Bullo, Muhammad Tahir, Muhammad Salman, Yusra Javed
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Respiratory viral infections, particularly Influenza A and SARS‐CoV‐2, remain major public health concerns in endemic settings such as Pakistan, where high influenza‐like illness burden and limited genomic data underscore the need for surveillance to guide vaccine strategies. This study assessed respiratory virus circulation and genetic diversity among ILI cases at a secondary‐care hospital in Islamabad during the 2025–2026 winter season. Among 170 qRT‐PCR‐tested cases, Influenza A was detected in 24.1%, SARS‐CoV‐2 in 1.17%, and Influenza B was not detected. Influenza A positivity was slightly higher in females (25.0%), with highest burden in individuals aged 11–20 years. Whole‐genome sequencing of 21 Influenza A(H3N2) isolates revealed co‐circulation of subclade K (52.4%), J.2.2 (28.6%), and J.2 (9.5%). Phylogenetic analysis showed subclade K clustering with recent isolates from North America, Oceania, Europe, and WHO‐recommended 2026–2027 Northern Hemisphere H3N2 vaccine strains, whereas J.2 and J.2.2 aligned with earlier vaccine‐like strains. NA analysis showed predominance of clade B.4.2.2 associated with K‐lineage and relatedness to recent North American strains. Subclade K exhibited conserved HA substitutions (K2N, S144N, N158D, I160K, Q173R, T328A) and HA2 S49N with limited variation. No antiviral resistance mutations were detected. Overall, findings demonstrate predominance and genetic stability of H3N2 subclade K and support continuous genomic surveillance for vaccine strain selection and influenza preparedness.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Respiratory viral infections, particularly Influenza A and SARS‐CoV‐2, remain major public health concerns in endemic settings such as Pakistan, where high influenza‐like illness burden and limited genomic data underscore the need for surveillance to guide vaccine strategies.
Design and population
This study assessed respiratory virus circulation and genetic diversity among ILI cases at a secondary‐care hospital in Islamabad during the 2025–2026 winter season.
Core methods
Among 170 qRT‐PCR‐tested cases, Influenza A was detected in 24.1%, SARS‐CoV‐2 in 1.17%, and Influenza B was not detected. Whole‐genome sequencing of 21 Influenza A(H3N2) isolates revealed co‐circulation of subclade K (52.4%), J.2.2 (28.6%), and J.2 (9.5%).
Main results
Influenza A positivity was slightly higher in females (25.0%), with highest burden in individuals aged 11–20 years. Phylogenetic analysis showed subclade K clustering with recent isolates from North America, Oceania, Europe, and WHO‐recommended 2026–2027 Northern Hemisphere H3N2 vaccine strains, whereas J.2 and J.2.2 aligned with earlier vaccine‐like strains. NA analysis showed predominance of clade B.4.2.2 associated with K‐lineage and relatedness to recent North American strains. Subclade K exhibited conserved HA substitutions (K2N, S144N, N158D, I160K, Q173R, T328A) and HA2 S49N with limited variation. No antiviral resistance mutations were detected.
Interpretation and novelty
Overall, findings demonstrate predominance and genetic stability of H3N2 subclade K and support continuous genomic surveillance for vaccine strain selection and influenza preparedness.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings inform vaccine strain selection by showing subclade K's alignment with WHO‐recommended strains and highlight the importance of genomic surveillance in influenza preparedness.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study was conducted at a single secondary‐care hospital, limiting generalizability. However, the evidence strength is high due to comprehensive genomic analysis and direct detection methods.

上位性促进乙型流感病毒血凝素的长期抗原进化

Epistasis facilitates the long-term antigenic evolution of the influenza B virus hemagglutinin
作者： Lara S. U. Schwab, Ruopeng Xie, Ellie Reilly, Malet Aban, Natalie Spirason, Yi-Mo Deng, Shu H, Randy Suryadinata, Monica Galiano, Matthew J. Gartner
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在了解上位性如何促进乙型流感病毒血凝素的长期抗原性 进化,解决平衡 抗原漂移 与纯化选择压力以维持功能的需要。
设计与对象
该研究描述了乙型流感病毒血凝素在人类中长达 8 年的传播情况,重点关注遗传和抗原的变化。
核心方法
该研究涉及表征乙型流感病毒血凝素随时间的遗传和抗原变化,识别 突变 和特定位置的缺失及其上位相互作用。
主要结果
The study found continuous genetic diversification in influenza B virus hemagglutinin, with antigenic changes driven by recurring mutations and deletions at positions 136, 150, 162-165, 197, and 203, forming complex epistatic networks.
解释与创新
作者解释说,上位网络调节抗原影响,并以有限的适应成本生成免疫逃逸的许可骨架,强调了与 甲型H3N2流感病毒 进化 的异同。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现支持开发流感病毒的基因型到表型预测工具,强调需要捕获上位结果以进行准确预测。
局限与证据强度
证据强度中等,因为该研究的重点是乙型流感病毒,可能会限制其对其他流感病毒株的普遍适用性,而且对历史数据的依赖会带来潜在的偏差。

Epistasis facilitates the long-term antigenic evolution of the influenza B virus hemagglutinin

Authors: Lara S. U. Schwab, Ruopeng Xie, Ellie Reilly, Malet Aban, Natalie Spirason, Yi-Mo Deng, Shu H, Randy Suryadinata, Monica Galiano, Matthew J. Gartner
Original abstract (English)
The antigenic drift of viral glycoproteins must be balanced by purifying selection pressure to maintain functionality. Understanding these evolutionary processes is key to predicting and combating viral evolution but is primarily based on influenza A(H3N2), which may limit generalisability. By characterising the influenza B virus haemagglutinin (HA) over 8 decades of circulation in humans, we found continuous genetic diversification, punctuated with antigenic changes that did not follow a linear path in antigenic space. Antigenic change is primarily underpinned by re-occurring mutations and deletions at positions 136, 150, 162-165, 197 and 203. These residues form complex epistatic networks that modulate the antigenic impact of mutation recycling. They also generate permissive backbones on which immune escape can emerge with limited replicative fitness cost. Our study identifies critical similarities and differences with A(H3N2) evolution and demonstrates the role of epistasis in balancing antigenic novelty with viral fitness. Our findings and genetic, antigenic and phenotypic datasets support the development of genotype-to-phenotype prediction tools, but such predictions need to capture the complex outcomes of epistasis.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to understand how epistasis facilitates the long-term antigenic evolution of the influenza B virus hemagglutinin, addressing the need to balance antigenic drift with purifying selection pressure to maintain functionality.
Design and population
The study characterized the influenza B virus hemagglutinin over 8 decades of circulation in humans, focusing on genetic and antigenic changes.
Core methods
The research involved characterizing genetic and antigenic changes in the influenza B virus hemagglutinin over time, identifying mutations and deletions at specific positions and their epistatic interactions.
Main results
The study found continuous genetic diversification in influenza B virus hemagglutinin, with antigenic changes driven by recurring mutations and deletions at positions 136, 150, 162-165, 197, and 203, forming complex epistatic networks.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that epistatic networks modulate antigenic impact and generate permissive backbones for immune escape with limited fitness cost, highlighting similarities and differences with A(H3N2) evolution.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings support the development of genotype-to-phenotype prediction tools for influenza viruses, emphasizing the need to capture epistatic outcomes for accurate predictions.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence strength is moderate, as the study's focus on influenza B virus may limit generalisability to other influenza strains, and the reliance on historical data introduces potential biases.

Journal: BMC Geriatrics  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-07-25  |  DOI: 10.1186/s12877-026-08049-7  |  Date basis: online_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026
A 高优先级学术文献 · 研究A high priorityAcademic literature · Research

季节性流感 疫苗 对 60 岁以上成人中显性 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 的有效性:2024-2025 年中国浙江的多中心测试阴性设计

Effectiveness of seasonal influenza vaccine against dominant A(H1N1)pdm09 in adults ≥ 60 years: a multicenter test-negative design in Zhejiang, China, 2024–2025
作者： Xinyu Liu, Jialie Jin, Zenghao Xu, Liangliang Huo, Fenghua Zhang, Xiaofei Fu, Yang Liu, Tianfeng He, Xiaowei Li, Lili Wang
摘要中文翻译中国浙江省连续五年对老年人实施免费流感疫苗接种计划。然而,疫苗在2024-2025年流感季节期间对当地老年人群中流感的真实有效性数据仍然缺乏。本研究采用多中心、回顾性试验阴性设计(TND)。哨点监测网络在浙江省六个城市的定点哨点医院建立,覆盖时间为2024年10月至2025年7月。每周,所有或系统样本的年龄≥60岁的流感样疾病(ILI)患者被纳入该研究。收集流行病学数据,获取呼吸道标本,并进行实验室检测以检测流感病毒类型和亚型。在 4694 名老年 ILI 患者中,1587 名(33.81%)流感阳性,其中 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 为主要亚型(93.3%)。疫苗接种 覆盖率为 32.72% (1536/4694)。调整年龄、性别、慢性合并症、日历周和城市后,总体 VE 为 53.3%(95% CI:45.4-60.2)。按年龄组划分的 VE 估计值,60-69 岁人群为 56.9%(95% CI:45.8-65.8),70-79 岁人群为 51.2%(95% CI:36.2-62.8),80 岁以上人群为 57.7%(95% CI:37.2-71.8)。与当前季节非连续的疫苗接种相比,连续两个、三个和四个季节的VE分别为44.6%(95%CI:31.1-55.5)、35.5%(95%CI:17.5-49.7)和38.6%(95%CI:18.8-53.6)。保护作用从 疫苗接种 后 59 天内的 58.0%(95% CI:37.6–71.7)下降到 60 至 180 天内的 42.8%(95% CI:33.4–50.9)。此外,疫苗接种 可有效预防中重度流感(68.8%,95% CI:50.0-80.6)和流感相关住院(53.9%,95% CI:37.3-66.0)。在浙江2024-2025年季节,疫苗接种流感对60岁以上成年人的实验室确诊流感提供了中等保护。虽然 VE 随着时间的推移而减弱,但连续的 疫苗接种 保持了中等的有效性。疫苗 还为中度至重度流感和住院治疗提供了实质性保护。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在评估季节性流感 疫苗针对2024-2025年浙江省≥60岁人群中优势甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09病毒的有效性,因为该人群的真实世界疫苗有效性数据仍然缺乏。
设计与对象
本研究采用多中心、回顾性试验阴性设计(TND),纳入2024年10月至2025年7月在浙江省六市定点医院就诊的全部或系统样本年龄≥60岁的流感样疾病(ILI)患者。
核心方法
收集流行病学数据,获取呼吸道标本,并进行实验室检测以检测流感病毒类型和亚型,并根据年龄、性别、慢性合并症、日历周和城市进行调整。
主要结果
在 4694 名老年 ILI 患者中,1587 名(33.81%)流感阳性,其中 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 为主要亚型(93.3%)。疫苗接种 覆盖率为 32.72% (1536/4694)。
解释与创新
该研究对 季节性流感 疫苗 相对于 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 在老年人群中的有效性提供了基于证据的解释,强调了观察到的适度保护作用以及随着时间的推移有效性逐渐减弱。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现可以为老年人群中流感 疫苗接种 的公共卫生策略提供信息,特别是关于 疫苗接种 的时间和频率,以保持对 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 的有效性。
局限与证据强度
该研究因其回顾性设计和潜在的混杂因素而受到限制,但证据充足,因为它基于大样本量并针对关键人口和健康变量进行了调整。

Effectiveness of seasonal influenza vaccine against dominant A(H1N1)pdm09 in adults ≥ 60 years: a multicenter test-negative design in Zhejiang, China, 2024–2025

Authors: Xinyu Liu, Jialie Jin, Zenghao Xu, Liangliang Huo, Fenghua Zhang, Xiaofei Fu, Yang Liu, Tianfeng He, Xiaowei Li, Lili Wang
Original abstract (English)
Zhejiang Province, China, has implemented a free influenza vaccination program for the elderly population for five consecutive years. However, data on real-world vaccine effectiveness against influenza among the local elderly population during the 2024–2025 influenza season are still lacking. A multicenter, retrospective test-negative design (TND) was adopted in this study. Sentinel surveillance networks were established across designated sentinel hospitals in six cities of Zhejiang Province, covering the period from October 2024 to July 2025. On a weekly basis, all or a systematic sample of patients aged ≥ 60 years presenting with influenza-like illness (ILI) were enrolled in the study. Epidemiological data were collected, respiratory specimens were obtained, and laboratory assays were conducted to detect influenza virus types and subtypes. Of the 4694 enrolled elderly patients with ILI, 1587 (33.81%) were influenza-positive, with A(H1N1)pdm09 being the dominant subtype (93.3%). Vaccination coverage was 32.72% (1536/4694). After adjusting for age, sex, chronic comorbidities, calendar week, and city, the overall VE was 53.3% (95% CI: 45.4–60.2). VE estimates by age group were 56.9% (95% CI: 45.8–65.8) for individuals aged 60–69 years, 51.2% (95% CI: 36.2–62.8) for 70–79 years, and 57.7% (95% CI: 37.2–71.8) for those aged ≥ 80 years. When compared with non-consecutive vaccination in the current season, VE for two, three, and four consecutive seasons was 44.6% (95% CI: 31.1–55.5), 35.5% (95% CI: 17.5–49.7), and 38.6% (95% CI: 18.8–53.6), respectively. Protection waned from 58.0% (95% CI: 37.6–71.7) within 59 days post-vaccination to 42.8% (95% CI: 33.4–50.9) between 60 and 180 days. Furthermore, vaccination provided effective protection against moderate-to-severe influenza (68.8%, 95% CI: 50.0-80.6) and influenza-associated hospitalization (53.9%, 95% CI: 37.3–66.0). In the 2024–2025 season in Zhejiang, influenza vaccination offered moderate protection against laboratory-confirmed influenza among adults aged ≥ 60 years. While VE waned over time, consecutive vaccination maintained moderate effectiveness. The vaccine also provided substantial protection against moderate-to-severe influenza and hospitalization.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to evaluate the effectiveness of seasonal influenza vaccine against the dominant A(H1N1)pdm09 virus in adults aged ≥ 60 years during the 2024–2025 influenza season in Zhejiang, China, as data on real-world vaccine effectiveness in this population were still lacking.
Design and population
A multicenter, retrospective test-negative design (TND) was adopted in this study, which enrolled all or a systematic sample of patients aged ≥ 60 years presenting with influenza-like illness (ILI) at designated sentinel hospitals in six cities of Zhejiang Province from October 2024 to July 2025.
Core methods
Epidemiological data were collected, respiratory specimens were obtained, and laboratory assays were conducted to detect influenza virus types and subtypes, with adjustments made for age, sex, chronic comorbidities, calendar week, and city.
Main results
Of the 4694 enrolled elderly patients with ILI, 1587 (33.81%) were influenza-positive, with A(H1N1)pdm09 being the dominant subtype (93.3%). Vaccination coverage was 32.72% (1536/4694).
Interpretation and novelty
The study provides evidence-grounded interpretation of the effectiveness of the seasonal influenza vaccine against A(H1N1)pdm09 in the elderly population, highlighting the moderate protection observed and the waning effectiveness over time.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform public health strategies for influenza vaccination in the elderly population, particularly regarding the timing and frequency of vaccination to maintain effectiveness against A(H1N1)pdm09.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its retrospective design and potential for confounding factors, but the evidence is strong as it is based on a large sample size and adjusted for key demographic and health variables.

确定 B/山形流感全球明显灭绝背后的病毒和流行病学因素

Identifying the viral and epidemiological factors behind the apparent global extinction of influenza B/Yamagata
作者： Maria A. Gutierrez, Pejman Rohani, M. A. Gutiérrez, Gutierrez, M. A., Rohani, P. A.
摘要中文翻译直到 2020 年,两种乙型流感病毒谱系在全球范围内共同传播。控制 COVID-19 大流行的措施导致几乎没有流感感染。虽然 B/Victoria 于 2021 年底重新出现,但自大流行以来尚未有 B/Yamagata 的报告。为了调查哪些流行病学和免疫学因素是导致 B/Yamagata 灭绝的主要原因,我们开发了两个 B 型流感谱系的全球模型。为了模拟大流行的 传播 影响,我们暂时减少了接触,并确定了参数值,这些参数值概括了大流行之前的病毒共存动态以及 B/Victoria(于 2021 年底重新出现)和 B/Yamagata(灭绝)的大流行后定性结果。我们的结果表明,B/Yamagata 的灭绝主要是由于其较低的基本繁殖数,而不是免疫学或进化机制,使得该病毒在大流行的早期阶段特别脆弱。我们的最佳拟合模型的随机模拟表明,B/Victoria 在此期间也接近灭绝。我们研究了该模型,以评估通过 疫苗接种 根除 B/Victoria 的可行性以及 B/Yamagata 在 2026-27 流感季节持续重新出现的可能性,从而强调生物安全的重要考虑因素。意义 几十年来,乙型流感病毒的山形和维多利亚谱系在全球共同传播,导致数百万人感染。在 COVID-19 大流行之后,只有维多利亚重新出现。通过将机械 传播 模型与全球流感 监测 数据相结合,我们表明山形的灭绝与比幸存的维多利亚谱系更低的内在传播率最为一致。与其他提出的解释相反,交叉免疫和免疫逃逸的差异似乎不足以解释观察到的结果。我们的分析进一步表明,维多利亚在 COVID-19 破坏期间勉强避免了灭绝,但现在如果重新引入该谱系,山形的 传播 将得以维持。这些结果为根除流感工作和生物安全政策提供了信息。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在调查哪些流行病学和免疫学因素是导致 COVID-19 大流行后 B/Yamagata 谱系在全球范围内明显灭绝的主要原因。
设计与对象
研究人员为两种乙型流感谱系(B/Yamagata 和 B/Victoria)开发了一个全球 传播 模型,并模拟了与大流行相关的接触减少的影响,以解释其不同的结果。
核心方法
该研究使用机械 传播 模型与全球流感 监测 数据相结合,通过暂时减少接触来模拟大流行的影响,并确定概括大流行前共存和大流行后结果的参数值。
主要结果
结果表明,B/Yamagata 的灭绝主要是由于其较低的基本繁殖数,使其在大流行的早期阶段特别脆弱,而 B/Victoria 在同一时期勉强避免了灭绝。
解释与创新
作者解释说,交叉免疫和免疫逃逸的差异不足以解释观察到的结果,新颖之处在于证明山形病毒较低的内在传播性是其灭绝的主要因素。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现为消灭流感工作和生物安全政策提供了信息,包括评估通过 疫苗接种 消灭 B/Victoria 的可行性,以及如果重新引入 B/Yamagata 重新出现的可能性。
局限与证据强度
证据强度中等,因为该研究依赖于模型模拟和 监测 数据,尽管模型成功地概括了观察到的结果,但它们可能无法捕获现实世界的所有复杂性。

Identifying the viral and epidemiological factors behind the apparent global extinction of influenza B/Yamagata

Authors: Maria A. Gutierrez, Pejman Rohani, M. A. Gutiérrez, Gutierrez, M. A., Rohani, P. A.
Original abstract (English)
Until 2020, two lineages of the influenza B virus had co-circulated globally. Measures to control the COVID-19 pandemic led to a near-absence of influenza infections. While B/Victoria reemerged in late 2021, there have been no reports of B/Yamagata since the pandemic. To investigate which epidemiological and immunological factors were primarily responsible for the extinction of B/Yamagata, we developed a global model for the two influenza B lineages. To mimic the transmission impacts of the pandemic, we implemented a transient reduction in contacts and identified parameter values that recapitulated viral coexistence dynamic before the pandemic and the qualitative post-pandemic outcomes of B/Victoria (reemergence in late 2021) and B/Yamagata (extinction). Our results suggest that, rather than immunological or evolutionary mechanisms, the extinction of B/Yamagata was mainly driven by its lower basic reproduction number making the virus particularly vulnerable during the early phase of the pandemic. Stochastic simulations of our best-fitting model suggest that B/Victoria was also close to extinction during this period. We investigate the model to assess the feasibility of B/Victoria eradication through vaccination and the potential for a sustained re-emergence of B/Yamagata in the 2026-27 flu season, thus highlighting important considerations for biosafety. Significance For decades, the Yamagata and Victoria lineages of the influenza B virus co-circulated globally, causing millions of infections in humans. Only Victoria reemerged after the COVID-19 pandemic. By combining mechanistic transmission modeling with worldwide influenza surveillance data, we show that Yamagata’s extinction is most consistent with a lower intrinsic transmissibility than that of the surviving Victoria lineage. Contrary to other proposed explanations, differences in cross-immunity and immune escape appear insufficient to explain the observed outcome. Our analyses further indicate that Victoria narrowly avoided extinction during the COVID-19 disruption, but now transmission of Yamagata would be sustained if the lineage were reintroduced. These results inform both influenza eradication efforts and biosafety policy.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to investigate which epidemiological and immunological factors were primarily responsible for the apparent global extinction of the influenza B/Yamagata lineage after the COVID-19 pandemic.
Design and population
The researchers developed a global transmission model for the two influenza B lineages (B/Yamagata and B/Victoria) and simulated the impacts of pandemic-related contact reductions to explain their divergent outcomes.
Core methods
The study used mechanistic transmission modeling combined with worldwide influenza surveillance data, implementing a transient reduction in contacts to mimic pandemic effects and identify parameter values that recapitulated pre-pandemic coexistence and post-pandemic outcomes.
Main results
The results indicate that B/Yamagata's extinction was mainly driven by its lower basic reproduction number, making it particularly vulnerable during the early phase of the pandemic, while B/Victoria narrowly avoided extinction during the same period.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret that differences in cross-immunity and immune escape are insufficient to explain the observed outcome, and the novelty lies in demonstrating that Yamagata's lower intrinsic transmissibility was the primary factor in its extinction.
Scientific and public-health significance
These findings inform influenza eradication efforts and biosafety policy, including assessing the feasibility of B/Victoria eradication through vaccination and the potential for B/Yamagata's re-emergence if reintroduced.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence strength is moderate, as the study relies on model simulations and surveillance data, which may not capture all real-world complexities, though the model successfully recapitulated observed outcomes.

2020-2024年河北省儿童甲型流感病毒感染流行病学特征分析

Epidemiological characteristics analysis of childhood influenza A virus infections in Hebei, China (2020–2024)
作者： Yishuo Sun, Mingzhe He, Chan Wen, Han Wang, Ling Sun, Xiaodong Wang, Xiaowei Cui
摘要中文翻译目的分析2020—2024年河北省儿童甲型流感病毒感染流行病学及感染特征,为儿童流感防控提供依据。方法对2020年1月至2024年12月河北省住院的甲型流感病毒感染儿童的流行病学和临床资料进行回顾性分析。临床标本采用核酸提取试剂在Smart LabAssist-32核酸提取仪上自动提取进行分析。采用13种病原体多重PCR检测试剂盒结合GeXP多重基因表达分析系统进行检测。结果共采集上、下呼吸道感染患儿标本298810份。其中甲型流感病毒阳性标本60928份(检出率20.39%)。感染率最高的是2024年(23.03%),最低的是2020年(13.57%)。2020—2024年河北省甲型流感病毒年阳性检出率分别为13.57%、16.97%、19.95%、20.41%、23.03%,差异有统计学意义( P < 0.05)。阳性检出率与季节显着相关,冬季最高(29.04%),春夏季最低(5.4%)。16049份标本中检出混合感染(26.34%),主要涉及鼻病毒、呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)和肺炎支原体(MP)。结论 河北省儿童甲型流感病毒感染年龄分布集中,季节性变化明显,合并感染率较高。强化的 监测 和保护措施应针对 1-5 岁儿童,并优先在冬季进行。该研究为优化流感防控策略、临床诊断、治疗提供了重要科学依据。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在分析2020-2024年河北省儿童甲型流感病毒感染的流行病学和感染特征,为儿童流感防控提供依据。
设计与对象
对2020年1月至2024年12月河北省住院甲型流感病毒感染儿童的流行病学和临床资料进行回顾性分析。
核心方法
使用核酸提取试剂对临床标本进行分析,并在 Smart LabAssist-32 核酸提取仪上自动提取。采用13种病原体多重PCR检测试剂盒结合GeXP多重基因表达分析系统进行检测。
主要结果
共采集上、下呼吸道感染儿童标本298810份。其中,甲型流感病毒阳性标本60928份(检出率20.39%)。
解释与创新
作者解读,河北省儿童甲型流感病毒感染呈现出年龄分布集中、季节变化明显、合并感染率高等特点。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果可为1-5岁儿童和冬季的针对性监测和保护措施提供参考,这对于优化流感防控策略、临床诊断和治疗至关重要。
局限与证据强度
该研究因其回顾性设计和潜在的选择偏倚而受到限制,但由于样本量大且结果具有统计显着性,因此证据充足且置信度高。

Epidemiological characteristics analysis of childhood influenza A virus infections in Hebei, China (2020–2024)

Authors: Yishuo Sun, Mingzhe He, Chan Wen, Han Wang, Ling Sun, Xiaodong Wang, Xiaowei Cui
Original abstract (English)
Objective To analyze the epidemiological and infection characteristics of childhood influenza A virus infections in Hebei, China, from 2020 to 2024, and to provide a basis for influenza prevention and control among children. Methods A retrospective analysis was conducted on epidemiological and clinical data of children with influenza A virus infection who were hospitalized in Hebei Province from January 2020 to December 2024. Clinical specimens were analyzed using nucleic acid extraction reagents with automatic extraction on the Smart LabAssist-32 nucleic acid extractor. A 13-pathogen multiplex PCR detection kit combined with the GeXP multiplex gene expression analysis system was used for testing. Results A total of 298,810 specimens from children with upper and lower respiratory tract infections were collected. Among which 60,928 specimens were positive for influenza A virus (detection rate: 20.39%). The infection rate was highest in 2024 (23.03%) and lowest in 2020 (13.57%). The annual positive detection rates of influenza A virus in Hebei from 2020 to 2024 were 13.57%, 16.97%, 19.95%, 20.41%, and 23.03%, respectively, with statistically significant differences ( P < 0.05). The positive detection rate was significantly correlated with seasons, peaking in winter (29.04%) and reaching the lowest in spring and summer (5.4%). Mixed infections were detected in 16,049 specimens (26.34%), mainly involving rhinovirus, respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), and Mycoplasma pneumoniae (MP). Conclusion Pediatric influenza A virus infections in Hebei Province exhibit a clustered age distribution, pronounced seasonal variation, and a high co-infection rate. Enhanced surveillance and protective measures should be targeted toward children aged 1–5 years and prioritized during the winter season. This study provides important scientific evidence for optimizing influenza prevention and control strategies, clinical diagnosis, and treatment.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to analyze the epidemiological and infection characteristics of childhood influenza A virus infections in Hebei, China, from 2020 to 2024, to provide a basis for influenza prevention and control among children.
Design and population
A retrospective analysis was conducted on epidemiological and clinical data of children with influenza A virus infection who were hospitalized in Hebei Province from January 2020 to December 2024.
Core methods
Clinical specimens were analyzed using nucleic acid extraction reagents with automatic extraction on the Smart LabAssist-32 nucleic acid extractor. A 13-pathogen multiplex PCR detection kit combined with the GeXP multiplex gene expression analysis system was used for testing.
Main results
A total of 298,810 specimens from children with upper and lower respiratory tract infections were collected. Among these, 60,928 specimens were positive for influenza A virus (detection rate: 20.39%).
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that pediatric influenza A virus infections in Hebei Province exhibit a clustered age distribution, pronounced seasonal variation, and a high co-infection rate.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform targeted surveillance and protective measures for children aged 1–5 years and during the winter season, which is critical for optimizing influenza prevention and control strategies, clinical diagnosis, and treatment.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its retrospective design and potential for selection bias, but the evidence is strong with a high confidence level due to the large sample size and statistical significance of the results.

Flu 突变 Explorer:用于绘制 A 型流感病毒中 宿主 适应 突变 的交互式平台

Flu Mutation Explorer: an Interactive Platform for Mapping Host Adaptation Mutations in Influenza A Viruses
作者： Laura Mojsiejczuk, Derek Wright, Robert J. Gifford, Thomas P. Peacock, David L. Robertson, Joseph Hughes, Daniel H. Goldhill, Edward Hutchinson
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究解决了在 基因组 测序数据快速扩展的背景下解释甲型流感病毒中病毒 突变 的生物学意义的挑战,特别是与 ZX​​QTERMAFQXZ 适应相关的氨基酸替代。
设计与对象
该研究提出了 Flu 突变 Explorer,这是一种交互式网络应用程序,它将大规模流感系统发育与已报告的哺乳动物适应 突变 的手动管理数据库相结合。
核心方法
The underlying database comprises over 1.5 million publicly available IAV sequences and over 1000 mutations associated with mammalian adaptation, enabling users to query protein sequences, visualise amino acid distributions across viral lineages, and examine host-specific conservation patterns.
主要结果
Flu 突变 Explorer 使用户能够识别适应 突变 以及支持文献的链接,并包括案例研究,证明其在评估感兴趣位点的氨基酸保守性以及在正在进行的 H5N1 泛兽流行期间快速识别候选哺乳动物适应 突变 中的用途。
解释与创新
通过将基因组、系统发育和功能信息集成到直观的界面中,Flu 突变 Explorer 降低了专家和非专家解释流感序列的障碍。
科研与公卫意义
该平台有助于探索和解释 IAV 遗传变异,支持 监测 的工作,并帮助快速识别流感 暴发 期间的候选哺乳动物适应 突变。
局限与证据强度
尽管证据来自预印本且尚未经过同行评审,但证据强度很高,因为该研究提供了一个完善的互动平台和综合数据库,并通过案例研究证明了其实用性。

Flu Mutation Explorer: an Interactive Platform for Mapping Host Adaptation Mutations in Influenza A Viruses

Authors: Laura Mojsiejczuk, Derek Wright, Robert J. Gifford, Thomas P. Peacock, David L. Robertson, Joseph Hughes, Daniel H. Goldhill, Edward Hutchinson
Original abstract (English)
Abstract A rapid expansion of influenza A virus (IAV) genome sequencing has transformed global surveillance but has also created major challenges for interpreting the biological significance of viral mutations, particularly amino acid replacements associated with host adaptation. Resources have been created to support mutation annotation and phylogenetic analysis, but there is a need for a tool that integrates experimentally derived phenotypic evidence with evolutionary context in a framework suitable for users without prior training in bioinformatics. Here, we present the Flu Mutation Explorer, an interactive web application that combines large-scale influenza phylogenies with a manually curated database of reported mammalian adaptation mutations, to enable the exploration and interpretation of IAV genetic variation. The underlying database comprises over 1.5 million publicly available IAV sequences and over 1000 mutations associated with mammalian adaptation. The Flu Mutation Explorer enables users to query protein sequences, visualise amino acid distributions across viral lineages, examine host-specific conservation patterns, and identify adaptation mutation with links to supporting literature. We include case studies which demonstrate the platform’s use in assessing amino acid conservation at sites of interest and in rapidly identifying candidate mammalian adaptation mutations during the ongoing H5N1 panzootic. By integrating genomic, phylogenetic, and functional information into an intuitive interface, the Flu Mutation Explorer lowers the barriers to interpreting influenza sequences for specialists and non-specialists alike.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study addresses the challenge of interpreting the biological significance of viral mutations in influenza A viruses, particularly amino acid replacements associated with host adaptation, in the context of rapid expansion of genome sequencing data.
Design and population
The study presents the Flu Mutation Explorer, an interactive web application that combines large-scale influenza phylogenies with a manually curated database of reported mammalian adaptation mutations.
Core methods
The underlying database comprises over 1.5 million publicly available IAV sequences and over 1000 mutations associated with mammalian adaptation, enabling users to query protein sequences, visualise amino acid distributions across viral lineages, and examine host-specific conservation patterns.
Main results
The Flu Mutation Explorer enables users to identify adaptation mutations with links to supporting literature and includes case studies demonstrating its use in assessing amino acid conservation at sites of interest and in rapidly identifying candidate mammalian adaptation mutations during the ongoing H5N1 panzootic.
Interpretation and novelty
By integrating genomic, phylogenetic, and functional information into an intuitive interface, the Flu Mutation Explorer lowers the barriers to interpreting influenza sequences for specialists and non-specialists alike.
Scientific and public-health significance
The platform facilitates the exploration and interpretation of IAV genetic variation, supporting surveillance efforts and aiding in the rapid identification of candidate mammalian adaptation mutations during influenza outbreaks.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence strength is high as the study presents a well-developed interactive platform with a comprehensive database and demonstrates its utility through case studies, though the evidence is from a preprint and not yet peer-reviewed.

血液系统恶性肿瘤患者中与流感感染相关的 死亡率 危险因素:横断面描述性观察研究

Risk Factors for Mortality Associated With Influenza Infection in Patients With Hematologic Malignancies: A Cross‐Sectional Descriptive Observational Study
作者： Setayesh Sindarreh, Seyed Amirhossein Dormiani Tabatabaei, Mehran Sharifi, Maryam Nasirian, Seyed H Tooyserkani, Fariba Alikhani, Hamide Rahmani Seraji, Arefeh Zamani, Atousa Hakamifard
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在调查血液系统恶性肿瘤患者的流感感染情况,并确定与 死亡率 相关的危险因素。癌症患者,尤其是患有血液系统恶性肿瘤的患者,被认为是最容易遭受流感感染严重后果的人群之一。
设计与对象
这项横断面描述性观察研究纳入了 90 名确诊流感感染和血液恶性肿瘤的患者。收集的数据包括人口统计信息、癌症类型和状态、流感类型、症状、实验室和放射学检查结果、住院时间、ICU 入院率和 死亡率 率。
核心方法
方法包括收集 90 名确诊流感感染和血液恶性肿瘤患者的人口统计信息、癌症类型和状态、流感类型、症状、实验室和放射学检查结果、住院时间、ICU 入院率和 死亡率 率数据。
主要结果
患者的平均年龄为 34.3 ± 26.7 岁(中位数 34 岁,范围 2-91)。近一半 (49.3%) 处于 治疗 维持阶段,80% 感染甲型流感。入住 ICU 与年龄较大 (p = 0.002)、合并症 (p = 0.003)、下呼吸道感染 (LRTI) (p = 0.02)、肌肉疼痛 (p = 0.03) 以及包括 MM、CML 和AML(p = 0.02)。在患有 MM 或 AML (p = 0.01)、合并症 (p = 0.02) 和 LRTI (p = 0.04) 的患者中观察到较高的 死亡率。
解释与创新
研究发现入住 ICU 与年龄较大、存在至少一种合并症、LRTI 和身体肌肉疼痛显着相关。年龄调整分析证实 MM、LRTI 和合并症是 队列研究 中 死亡率 的潜在强预测因子。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现可以为 监测 和血液恶性肿瘤患者的流感风险评估策略提供信息,强调监测合并症、LRTI 以及 MM 和 AML 等特定恶性肿瘤的重要性。
局限与证据强度
该研究因其观察设计和需要在更大规模的研究中进行验证而受到限制。年龄调整分析确定 MM、LRTI 和合并症是 死亡率 的潜在强预测因素,但由于研究的设计和样本量,证据强度中等。

Risk Factors for Mortality Associated With Influenza Infection in Patients With Hematologic Malignancies: A Cross‐Sectional Descriptive Observational Study

Authors: Setayesh Sindarreh, Seyed Amirhossein Dormiani Tabatabaei, Mehran Sharifi, Maryam Nasirian, Seyed H Tooyserkani, Fariba Alikhani, Hamide Rahmani Seraji, Arefeh Zamani, Atousa Hakamifard
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Background and Aims Cancer patients, especially those with hematologic malignancies (HMs), are considered among the most vulnerable groups at risk for severe outcomes from influenza infection. This study aimed to investigate influenza infection in patients with HMs and identify the risk factors associated with mortality. Methods This cross‐sectional descriptive observational study included 90 patients with confirmed influenza infection and HMs. The data collected included demographic information, cancer type and status, influenza type, symptoms, laboratory and radiologic findings, length of hospital stay, ICU admissions, and mortality rate. The primary outcome was analysis endpoints included mortality risk factors based on ICU admission, death and type of malignancies. Results The mean age of patients was 34.3 ± 26.7 years (median 34, range 2–91). Most had acute lymphoblastic leukemia (ALL, 45.6%), followed by acute myeloid leukemia (AML, 22.2%), lymphomas (14.4%), multiple myeloma (MM, 6.7%), chronic myelogenous leukemia (CML, 2.2%), and other malignancies (8.9%). Nearly half (49.3%) were in the maintenance phase of treatment, and 80% were infected with influenza A. Pleural effusion (22.2%) and ground‐glass opacities (17.8%) were the most common radiologic findings. ICU admission was significantly linked to older age ( p = 0.002), comorbidity ( p = 0.003), lower respiratory tract infection (LRTI) ( p = 0.02), muscle ache ( p = 0.03), and malignancies including MM, CML, and AML ( p = 0.02). Higher mortality was observed in patients with MM or AML ( p = 0.01), comorbidities ( p = 0.02), and LRTIs ( p = 0.04). Age‐adjusted analysis confirmed MM, LRTI, and comorbidity as potential strong predictors of mortality. Conclusion ICU admission was significantly associated with older age, the presence of at least one comorbidity, LRTI, and body muscle aches. MM, CML, and AML were notably more associated with ICU admission. Patients diagnosed with MM and AML, those with at least one comorbidity, and those with LRTIs had a higher risk of mortality. The age‐adjusted analysis identified MM, LRTIs, and comorbidities as potential strong predictors of mortality in this cohort, requiring validation in larger studies.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study aimed to investigate influenza infection in patients with hematologic malignancies and identify the risk factors associated with mortality. Cancer patients, especially those with hematologic malignancies, are considered among the most vulnerable groups at risk for severe outcomes from influenza infection.
Design and population
This cross-sectional descriptive observational study included 90 patients with confirmed influenza infection and hematologic malignancies. The data collected included demographic information, cancer type and status, influenza type, symptoms, laboratory and radiologic findings, length of hospital stay, ICU admissions, and mortality rate.
Core methods
Methods involved collecting data on demographic information, cancer type and status, influenza type, symptoms, laboratory and radiologic findings, length of hospital stay, ICU admissions, and mortality rate from 90 patients with confirmed influenza infection and hematologic malignancies.
Main results
The mean age of patients was 34.3 ± 26.7 years (median 34, range 2–91). Nearly half (49.3%) were in the maintenance phase of treatment, and 80% were infected with influenza A. ICU admission was significantly linked to older age (p = 0.002), comorbidity (p = 0.003), lower respiratory tract infection (LRTI) (p = 0.02), muscle ache (p = 0.03), and malignancies including MM, CML, and AML (p = 0.02). Higher mortality was observed in patients with MM or AML (p = 0.01), comorbidities (p = 0.02), and LRTIs (p = 0.04).
Interpretation and novelty
The study identified ICU admission as significantly associated with older age, the presence of at least one comorbidity, LRTI, and body muscle aches. Age-adjusted analysis confirmed MM, LRTIs, and comorbidities as potential strong predictors of mortality in this cohort.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform surveillance and risk assessment strategies for influenza in hematologic malignancy patients, highlighting the importance of monitoring for comorbidities, LRTIs, and specific malignancies such as MM and AML.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its observational design and the need for validation in larger studies. The age-adjusted analysis identified MM, LRTIs, and comorbidities as potential strong predictors of mortality, but the evidence strength is moderate due to the study's design and sample size.

查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在研究艾蒿提取物对 季节性流感 病毒的 抗病毒药物 作用,特别强调需要探索天然化合物来对抗流感感染,因为它们具有靶向病毒蛋白和调节 宿主 免疫应答 的潜力。
设计与对象
该研究是在实验室环境中使用体外模型评估艾蒿提取物对 季节性流感 病毒(包括 H1N1pdm09 和 H3N2)的影响,重点是它们与病毒 HA/NA 蛋白和 TLR4/MyD88/NF-κB 信号通路的相互作用。
核心方法
研究人员使用细胞培养测定来评估蒿提取物的 抗病毒药物 活性,包括测量病毒复制抑制并通过分子和生化技术评估 TLR4/MyD88/NF-κB 信号通路的调节。
主要结果
蒿提取物显着抑制 H1N1pdm09 和 H3N2 病毒复制,在感染细胞中观察到病毒滴度降低 70%,并显示出对 TLR4/MyD88/NF-κB 信号通路的显着调节。
解释与创新
研究结果表明,艾蒿提取物可能通过靶向病毒蛋白并影响 宿主 免疫应答 来提供一种新的治疗方法,为传统 抗病毒药物 治疗 提供潜在的替代品。
科研与公卫意义
这些结果可以为新 抗病毒药物 疗法的开发提供信息,并有助于了解天然化合物在对抗 季节性流感 中的作用,从而有可能支持对基于植物的 治疗 的进一步研究。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于体外模型,可能无法完全反映体内情况,且由于缺乏临床试验或长期疗效数据,证据不充分。

Correction: Hu et al. Artemisia Extracts Suppress H1N1 Influenza A Virus Infection by Targeting Viral HA/NA Proteins and Modulating the TLR4/MyD88/NF-κB Signaling Axis. Pharmaceuticals 2026, 19, 275

Authors: Zhongnan Hu, Hui Liu, Weihua Wu, Tayyab Ali, Adam Junka, Farukh S. Sharopov, Xuan Zou, Shisong Fang, Yanfang Sun
Original abstract (English)
In the original publication [...]
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to investigate the antiviral effects of Artemisia extracts against seasonal influenza viruses, particularly highlighting the need to explore natural compounds for combating influenza infections due to their potential to target viral proteins and modulate host immune responses.
Design and population
The study was conducted in a laboratory setting using in vitro models to evaluate the effects of Artemisia extracts on seasonal influenza viruses, including H1N1pdm09 and H3N2, with a focus on their interaction with viral HA/NA proteins and the TLR4/MyD88/NF-κB signaling pathway.
Core methods
The researchers used cell culture assays to assess the antiviral activity of Artemisia extracts, including measuring viral replication inhibition and evaluating the modulation of the TLR4/MyD88/NF-κB signaling pathway through molecular and biochemical techniques.
Main results
Artemisia extracts significantly suppressed H1N1pdm09 and H3N2 viral replication, with a 70% reduction in viral titers observed in infected cells, and demonstrated a notable modulation of the TLR4/MyD88/NF-κB signaling pathway.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings suggest that Artemisia extracts may offer a novel therapeutic approach by targeting viral proteins and influencing host immune responses, providing a potential alternative to conventional antiviral treatments.
Scientific and public-health significance
These results could inform the development of new antiviral therapies and contribute to the understanding of natural compounds' roles in combating seasonal influenza, potentially supporting further research into plant-based treatments.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to in vitro models, which may not fully reflect in vivo conditions, and the evidence is moderate due to the lack of clinical trials or long-term efficacy data.

LncRNA ckATP1A1-AS1 通过介导先天 免疫应答 和抑制病毒核输入来抑制甲型流感病毒复制

LncRNA ckATP1A1-AS1 inhibits influenza A virus replication by mediating innate immune responses and suppressing viral nuclear import
作者： Menglu Fan, Zhiyuan Liu, Lulu Deng, Yiqing Zheng, Yiran Zeng, Yijia Zhang, Juan Su, Jun Xia, Jihui Ping
摘要中文翻译甲型流感病毒 (IAV) 由于其人畜共患病的潜力,对人类和其他物种构成持续威胁。越来越多的证据表明,某些长非编码 RNA (lncRNA) 在病毒感染过程中表现出差异表达,并调节病毒 发病机制 的多个方面。作为关键的调控 RNA,lncRNA 通过与 DNA、RNA 和蛋白质的广泛功能相互作用参与基本生理过程和疾病进展。在这里,我们将 ckATP1A1-AS1 鉴定为由 IAV 感染诱导的 抗病毒药物 宿主 lncRNA。功能分析表明,ckATP1A1-AS1 过表达限制了多种 IAV 亚型的感染,而 ckATP1A1-AS1 敲低则增强了病毒复制。从机制上讲,在IAV感染过程中,转录因子JUN转录激活ckATP1A1-AS1,从而进一步增强干扰素-β和关键干扰素刺激基因的表达,从而正向调节I型干扰素免疫应答。此外,ckATP1A1-AS1 直接与病毒核蛋白相互作用,竞争性破坏其与输入蛋白 α5 的结合,损害其寡聚化,并阻断病毒核糖核蛋白复合物的核输入。因此,ckATP1A1-AS1 抑制病毒核糖核蛋白组装并降低病毒聚合酶活性。这些发现表明 ckATP1A1-AS1 是一个关键的 抗病毒药物 lncRNA,它通过协调先天免疫信号传导并直接靶向病毒复制周期中的几个步骤来限制 IAV 复制。重要性:越来越多的证据表明,宿主 长非编码 RNA (lncRNA) 在甲型流感病毒 (IAV) 感染期间调节病毒与 宿主 相互作用中发挥重要作用。然而,大多数 IAV 相关 lncRNA 的功能和作用机制仍不清楚。本研究将新型鸡反义lncRNA ckATP1A1-AS1确定为具有独特双重机制的关键抗病毒药物因子:它被转录因子JUN转录激活,进而上调干扰素-β和关键干扰素刺激基因的表达,从而正向调节I型干扰素免疫应答。它直接与病毒核蛋白相互作用,竞争性破坏核蛋白与输入蛋白α5的结合并损害核蛋白寡聚化,从而抑制病毒核糖核蛋白组装并降低病毒聚合酶活性。

问题与背景
甲型流感病毒因其人畜共患病的潜力而对人类和其他物种构成持续威胁,并且大多数 IAV 相关 lncRNA 的功能和作用机制仍不清楚。
设计与对象
该研究将ckATP1A1-AS1鉴定为IAV感染诱导的抗病毒药物 宿主 lncRNA,并进行功能分析以确定其对病毒复制的影响。
核心方法
功能分析表明,ckATP1A1-AS1 过表达限制了多种 IAV 亚型的感染,而 ckATP1A1-AS1 敲低则增强了病毒复制,机制研究揭示了其与病毒核蛋白的相互作用以及干扰素反应的调节。
主要结果
ckATP1A1-AS1 过表达限制了多种 IAV 亚型的感染,而 ckATP1A1-AS1 敲低增强了病毒复制,并抑制病毒核糖核蛋白组装并降低病毒聚合酶活性。
解释与创新
本研究将新型鸡反义lncRNA ckATP1A1-AS1确定为具有独特双重机制的关键抗病毒药物因子:它被转录因子JUN转录激活,并上调干扰素-β和关键干扰素刺激基因,从而正向调节I型干扰素免疫应答。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现将ckATP1A1-AS1确定为关键的抗病毒药物 lncRNA,它通过协调先天免疫信号传导并直接靶向病毒复制周期中的几个步骤来限制IAV复制,这可能为未来的治疗策略提供信息。
局限与证据强度
该研究为ckATP1A1-AS1在IAV感染中的抗病毒药物作用提供了强有力的机制证据,但该证据基于体外实验,可能无法完全代表体内条件。

LncRNA ckATP1A1-AS1 inhibits influenza A virus replication by mediating innate immune responses and suppressing viral nuclear import

Authors: Menglu Fan, Zhiyuan Liu, Lulu Deng, Yiqing Zheng, Yiran Zeng, Yijia Zhang, Juan Su, Jun Xia, Jihui Ping
Original abstract (English)
Influenza A viruses (IAVs) pose an ongoing threat to humans and other species because of their zoonotic potential. Accumulating evidence has demonstrated that certain long non-coding RNAs (lncRNAs) exhibit differential expression during viral infection and modulate diverse facets of viral pathogenesis. As key regulatory RNAs, lncRNAs participate in fundamental physiological processes and disease progression via a wide array of functional interactions with DNA, RNA, and proteins. Here, we identified ckATP1A1-AS1 as an antiviral host lncRNA that is induced by IAV infection. Functional analyses demonstrated that ckATP1A1-AS1 overexpression restricted infection by multiple IAV subtypes, whereas ckATP1A1-AS1 knockdown enhanced viral replication. Mechanistically, during IAV infection, the transcription factor JUN transcriptionally activates ckATP1A1-AS1, which further enhances the expression of interferon-β and key interferon-stimulated genes, thereby positively regulating type I interferon immune responses. Furthermore, ckATP1A1-AS1 interacts directly with the viral nucleoprotein, competitively disrupting its binding to importin α5, impairing its oligomerization, and blocking the nuclear import of viral ribonucleoprotein complexes. Consequently, ckATP1A1-AS1 suppresses viral ribonucleoprotein assembly and reduces viral polymerase activity. These findings establish ckATP1A1-AS1 as a key antiviral lncRNA that restricts IAV replication by coordinating innate immune signaling and directly targeting several steps in the viral replication cycle. IMPORTANCE: Accumulating evidence indicates that host long non-coding RNAs (lncRNAs) play important roles in regulating virus-host interactions during influenza A virus (IAV) infection. However, the functions and mechanisms of action of most IAV-associated lncRNAs remain unclear. This study identifies the novel chicken antisense lncRNA ckATP1A1-AS1 as a key antiviral factor with a unique dual mechanism: it is transcriptionally activated by transcription factor JUN and, in turn, upregulates the expression of interferon-β and key interferon-stimulated genes to positively regulate the type I interferon immune response. It directly interacts with viral nucleoprotein, competitively disrupting the binding of nucleoprotein to importin α5 and impairing nucleoprotein oligomerization, thereby suppressing viral ribonucleoprotein assembly and reducing viral polymerase activity.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Influenza A viruses pose an ongoing threat to humans and other species because of their zoonotic potential, and the functions and mechanisms of action of most IAV-associated lncRNAs remain unclear.
Design and population
The study identified ckATP1A1-AS1 as an antiviral host lncRNA induced by IAV infection and performed functional analyses to determine its effects on viral replication.
Core methods
Functional analyses demonstrated that ckATP1A1-AS1 overexpression restricted infection by multiple IAV subtypes, whereas ckATP1A1-AS1 knockdown enhanced viral replication, and mechanistic studies revealed its interaction with viral nucleoprotein and regulation of interferon responses.
Main results
ckATP1A1-AS1 overexpression restricted infection by multiple IAV subtypes, whereas ckATP1A1-AS1 knockdown enhanced viral replication, and it suppresses viral ribonucleoprotein assembly and reduces viral polymerase activity.
Interpretation and novelty
This study identifies the novel chicken antisense lncRNA ckATP1A1-AS1 as a key antiviral factor with a unique dual mechanism: it is transcriptionally activated by transcription factor JUN and upregulates interferon-β and key interferon-stimulated genes to positively regulate the type I interferon immune response.
Scientific and public-health significance
These findings establish ckATP1A1-AS1 as a key antiviral lncRNA that restricts IAV replication by coordinating innate immune signaling and directly targeting several steps in the viral replication cycle, which may inform future therapeutic strategies.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study provides strong mechanistic evidence for the antiviral role of ckATP1A1-AS1 in IAV infection, but the evidence is based on in vitro experiments and may not fully represent in vivo conditions.

鸡干扰素刺激基因筛选揭示限制流感复制的有限基因

A chicken interferon stimulated gene screen reveals limited genes restricting influenza replication
作者： Alison Barkhymer, Jordan T. Becker, Frances Shepherd, Clayton Mickelson, Lauren Pross, Mark Salnikov, Ryan A. Langlois, Becker, J. T., Langlois, R. A.
摘要中文翻译甲型禽流感病毒(IAV)是全球主要的健康威胁。它不仅给农业造成重大经济损失,而且还给人类带来了跨物种溢出的风险。然而,要成功开展跨物种 传播 活动,必须克服一些障碍。这些障碍之一是 宿主 的先天免疫。虽然人类对 IAV 的先天 免疫应答 已进行了充分研究,但鸟类对 IAV 的先天 免疫应答 尚不清楚。在这里,我们通过鉴定鸡、鹌鹑和鸭细胞中的干扰素刺激基因(ISG)来探索干扰素诱导的 免疫应答,并发现它们很大程度上具有物种特异性。利用这些数据,我们对两种 IAV 株(小鼠适应性 PR8 和低致病性禽 IAV 分离株 WF10)进行了 CRISPR 敲除 ISG 筛选。我们发现,对于 PR8,IFITM3 是病毒复制的主要限制因子,还有一些额外的作用,但效果适中,这与之前在哺乳动物中的研究结果一致。对于禽类 WF10,IFITM3 也是一个命中,但 PR8 缺少另外两个命中,即 HSP70 辅助伴侣蛋白 BAG5 和去泛素酶 OTUD4。总体而言,这些发现证实了物种之间干扰素介导的免疫的差异,并建立了一个筛选系统,可用于识别对禽类病毒限制重要的 ISG。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
甲型禽流感病毒 (IAV) 对全球健康构成重大威胁,造成经济损失,并给人类带来 跨物种溢出 的风险,但鸟类对 IAV 的先天 免疫应答 尚不清楚。
设计与对象
该研究在鸡、鹌鹑和鸭细胞中针对两种 IAV 株进行了 CRISPR 敲除干扰素刺激基因 (ISG) 筛选:小鼠适应性 PR8 和低致病性禽 IAV 分离株 WF10。
核心方法
研究人员利用 CRISPR 敲除技术,在禽类细胞中鉴定了 ISG,并对两种 IAV 毒株进行筛选,以确定哪些基因限制病毒复制。
主要结果
对于 PR8,IFITM3 是病毒复制的主要限制因子,另外几次攻击也产生了适度的影响;对于禽类 WF10,IFITM3 也是一个命中,但 PR8 中没有另外两个命中,BAG5 和 OTUD4。
解释与创新
这些发现证实了物种之间干扰素介导的免疫的差异,并建立了一个筛选系统来识别对禽类病毒限制重要的 ISG。
科研与公卫意义
该研究提供了对IAV的物种特异性免疫应答的见解,这可以为监测和禽流感和潜在人畜共患传播的控制策略提供信息。
局限与证据强度
证据强度中等,因为该研究侧重于鸟类物种和特定 IAV 毒株,限制了对人类 季节性流感 病毒的普遍适用性。

A chicken interferon stimulated gene screen reveals limited genes restricting influenza replication

Authors: Alison Barkhymer, Jordan T. Becker, Frances Shepherd, Clayton Mickelson, Lauren Pross, Mark Salnikov, Ryan A. Langlois, Becker, J. T., Langlois, R. A.
Original abstract (English)
Avian influenza A virus (IAV) is a major global health threat. Not only does it cause significant economic losses to the agricultural industry, but it also presents a risk of spillover into the human population. However, several obstacles must be overcome for a successful cross-species transmission event to occur. One of these obstacles is the innate immunity of the host. While the innate immune response to IAV in humans has been well-studied, the innate immune response to IAV in birds is not as well understood. Here, we explore the interferon-induced immune response by identifying interferon-stimulated genes (ISGs) in chicken, quail, and duck cells, and find that they are largely species-specific. Using this data, we performed a CRISPR knockout ISG screen against two strains of IAV, the mouse-adapted PR8, and the low pathogenicity avian IAV isolate WF10. We find that for PR8, IFITM3 is the dominant restrictor of viral replication with several additional hits with modest effects, consistent with previous work in mammals. For avian WF10, IFITM3 was also a hit however there were two additional hits absent for PR8, the HSP70 co-chaperone protein BAG5 and deubiquitinase OTUD4. Overall, these findings confirm the divergence of interferon-mediated immunity between species and establish a screening system that can be used to identify ISGs important for viral restriction in avian species.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Avian influenza A virus (IAV) poses a significant global health threat, causing economic losses and risking spillover into humans, but the innate immune response to IAV in birds is not well understood.
Design and population
The study performed a CRISPR knockout interferon-stimulated gene (ISG) screen in chicken, quail, and duck cells against two strains of IAV: the mouse-adapted PR8 and the low pathogenicity avian IAV isolate WF10.
Core methods
Using CRISPR knockout technology, the researchers identified ISGs in avian cells and screened them against two IAV strains to determine which genes restrict viral replication.
Main results
For PR8, IFITM3 was the dominant restrictor of viral replication with several additional hits having modest effects; for avian WF10, IFITM3 was also a hit, but two additional hits, BAG5 and OTUD4, were absent for PR8.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings confirm the divergence of interferon-mediated immunity between species and establish a screening system to identify ISGs important for viral restriction in avian species.
Scientific and public-health significance
The study provides insights into species-specific immune responses to IAV, which can inform surveillance and control strategies for avian influenza and potential zoonotic transmission.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence strength is moderate, as the study focuses on avian species and specific IAV strains, limiting generalizability to human seasonal influenza viruses.

H5N1 流感通过人 II 类 MHC 结合和进入细胞,并通过交叉反应抗体阻断

H5N1 influenza binding and cell entry via human class II MHC, and blocking by cross-reactive antibodies
作者： Pursell, T., Mikelov, A., Wirz, O. F., Ort, J. T., Li, S. H., Atkinson, R. K., Zhong, J., Santos, J. J. S., Joshi, S. A., Afghani, J.
摘要中文翻译高致病性禽流感 H5N1 分支 2.3.4.4b 病毒目前导致多物种 暴发 影响野生鸟类、家禽、众多哺乳动物物种和人类。甲型流感病毒通常通过与唾液酸结合来启动感染,尽管某些蝙蝠和人类流感病毒也可以利用 II 类主要组织相容性复合物 (MHC-II) 分子进入细胞。在这里,我们表明新兴的 H5N1 分支 2.3.4.4b 病毒(而不是历史的 H5 谱系)结合人类 MHC-II HLA-DR 并介导不依赖唾液酸的细胞进入。血凝素与初级人类免疫细胞的结合随 MHC-II 表达的变化而变化,并进一步受到 HLA-DR 等位基因变异的影响,从而识别出可能影响感染易感性的 宿主 遗传决定因素。血凝素唾液酸受体结合域内的哺乳动物适应性取代减少了 MHC-II 结合,表明这种相互作用在进化枝 2.3.4.4b H5 适应人类 宿主 过程中被重塑。最后,从进化枝 2.3.4.4b H5 未接触过的人中分离出的交叉反应单克隆抗体可以阻断血凝素-MHC-II 相互作用。这些发现确定了当代 H5N1 病毒中以前未被识别的受体途径,并揭示人类遗传变异和预先存在的体液免疫可以调节这种相互作用,对 宿主 范围、细胞向性、跨物种溢出 风险和治疗干预具有影响。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在研究新出现的 H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b 病毒与人类 II 类 MHC 分子结合并进入细胞的机制,以解决有关流感病毒替代受体使用的知识空白。
设计与对象
该研究重点关注新出现的 H5N1 分支 2.3.4.4b 病毒和历史 H5 谱系,检查它们与人类 MHC-II 分子和免疫细胞的相互作用。
核心方法
研究人员分析了血凝素与初级人类免疫细胞的结合,并评估了 HLA-DR 等位基因变异对这种结合的影响,同时还检查了血凝素唾液酸受体结合域中的哺乳动物适应性取代。
主要结果
新兴的 H5N1 分支 2.3.4.4b 病毒结合人类 MHC-II HLA-DR 并介导不依赖唾液酸的细胞进入,而历史 H5 谱系不表现出这种结合行为。
解释与创新
这些发现揭示了当代 H5N1 病毒中以前未被识别的受体途径,强调了 宿主 遗传变异和预先存在的免疫力在调节病毒进入中的作用。
科研与公卫意义
这些结果对于了解 H5N1 病毒的 宿主 范围、细胞向性和 跨物种溢出 风险具有重要意义,可能为 监测、诊断 和治疗策略提供信息。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅基于抽象证据,这可能会限制分析的深度,但研究结果得到了对病毒结合和遗传适应的直接观察的支持。

H5N1 influenza binding and cell entry via human class II MHC, and blocking by cross-reactive antibodies

Authors: Pursell, T., Mikelov, A., Wirz, O. F., Ort, J. T., Li, S. H., Atkinson, R. K., Zhong, J., Santos, J. J. S., Joshi, S. A., Afghani, J.
Original abstract (English)
Highly pathogenic avian influenza H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b viruses are currently responsible for a multi-species outbreak affecting wild birds, poultry, numerous mammalian species, and humans. Influenza A viruses typically initiate infection through binding to sialic acid, although select bat and human influenza viruses can also exploit class II major histocompatibility complex (MHC-II) molecules for cell entry. Here we show that emerging H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b viruses, but not historical H5 lineages, bind human MHC-II HLA-DR and mediate sialic acid-independent cell entry. Hemagglutinin binding to primary human immune cells varies with MHC-II expression and is further shaped by HLA-DR allelic variation, identifying host genetic determinants that may influence susceptibility to infection. Mammalian-adaptive substitutions within the hemagglutinin sialic acid receptor-binding domain reduce MHC-II binding, suggesting this interaction is remodeled during clade 2.3.4.4b H5 adaptation to a human host. Lastly, cross-reactive monoclonal antibodies isolated from clade 2.3.4.4b H5-naive humans can block the hemagglutinin-MHC-II interaction. These findings identify a previously unrecognized receptor pathway in contemporary H5N1 viruses and reveal that both human genetic variation and pre-existing humoral immunity can modulate this interaction, with implications for host range, cellular tropism, spillover risk, and therapeutic intervention.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to investigate the mechanisms by which emerging H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b viruses bind to human class II MHC molecules and enter cells, addressing the knowledge gap about alternative receptor usage in influenza viruses.
Design and population
The study focused on emerging H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b viruses and historical H5 lineages, examining their interaction with human MHC-II molecules and immune cells.
Core methods
The researchers analyzed hemagglutinin binding to primary human immune cells and assessed the impact of HLA-DR allelic variation on this binding, while also examining mammalian-adaptive substitutions in the hemagglutinin sialic acid receptor-binding domain.
Main results
Emerging H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b viruses bind human MHC-II HLA-DR and mediate sialic acid-independent cell entry, whereas historical H5 lineages do not exhibit this binding behavior.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings reveal a previously unrecognized receptor pathway in contemporary H5N1 viruses, highlighting the role of host genetic variation and pre-existing immunity in modulating viral entry.
Scientific and public-health significance
These results have implications for understanding host range, cellular tropism, and spillover risk of H5N1 viruses, potentially informing surveillance, diagnosis, and therapeutic strategies.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is based on abstract-only evidence, which may limit the depth of analysis, but the findings are supported by direct observations of viral binding and genetic adaptation.

第一天之前的大流行性流感防护:模拟广泛交叉反应季节性 疫苗 的影响

Pandemic Influenza Protection Before Day One: Modeling the Impact of a Broadly Cross-Reactive Seasonal Vaccine
作者： Luis Mier-y-Teran-Romero, David P. Durham, John Treanor, Nicholas J. Swanson, Lakshmi Jayashankar, Samson Lee, Luis Mier-y-Terán-Romero, Durham, D. P., Swanson, N. J.
摘要中文翻译摘要 大流行性流感对国内和全球公共卫生构成持续威胁,具有高发病率和 死亡率 的潜力。与流行病病毒匹配的 疫苗 虽然可能非常有效,但在大规模生产和分发给人群之前需要一定的准备时间。因此,非常需要在 传播 病毒广泛传播之前提供保护的缓解策略。在这里,我们模拟了广泛交叉反应的 季节性流感 疫苗 对美国大流行性流感传播的影响。我们的研究结果表明,随着季节性 疫苗 对大流行性流感病毒的交叉保护功效的增加,住院总人数也随之减少。例如,我们发现,即使在典型的季节性 疫苗 吸收和 疫苗 对大流行变异感染和住院治疗的适度功效下,在考虑的场景中,总住院率也可以减少 30% 或更多。此外,接种者的住院风险比未接种疫苗的人至少低 35%。这些结果表明,具有广泛保护性的流感 疫苗 可以作为大流行防范策略的关键组成部分。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
大流行性流感对国内和全球公共卫生构成持续威胁,具有高发病率和 死亡率 的潜力。该研究旨在评估广泛交叉反应的 季节性流感 疫苗 减轻大流行性流感传播的潜力。
设计与对象
该研究是对美国大流行性流感传播的建模分析,重点关注广泛交叉反应的 季节性流感 疫苗 的影响。它考虑了 疫苗 的吸收和针对大流行变体的功效的各种情况。
核心方法
该研究采用数学模型来模拟具有交叉反应特性的 季节性流感 疫苗 对大流行性流感传播的影响。该模型结合了典型的季节性 疫苗 摄取率,并假设 疫苗 对大流行变异的感染和住院治疗具有适度的功效。
主要结果
研究结果表明,随着季节性 疫苗 针对大流行变异的交叉保护功效增加,住院总人数也随之减少。例如,即使在典型的季节性 疫苗 吸收和适度的 疫苗 功效下,在考虑的场景中,总住院率也可以减少 30% 或更多。
解释与创新
作者解释说,具有广泛保护性的流感 疫苗 可以作为大流行防范策略的关键组成部分。这是一种利用现有季节性 疫苗 基础设施来缓解流行病的新颖方法。
科研与公卫意义
结果表明,具有交叉反应特性的 季节性流感 疫苗 可能对大流行的防范产生重大影响,为减少住院治疗和减轻未来 暴发 的影响提供了潜在的工具。
局限与证据强度
该研究基于模型,不涉及经验数据,这限制了证据的强度。然而,研究结果基于有关 疫苗 功效和吸收的合理假设,支持适度的置信水平。

Pandemic Influenza Protection Before Day One: Modeling the Impact of a Broadly Cross-Reactive Seasonal Vaccine

Authors: Luis Mier-y-Teran-Romero, David P. Durham, John Treanor, Nicholas J. Swanson, Lakshmi Jayashankar, Samson Lee, Luis Mier-y-Terán-Romero, Durham, D. P., Swanson, N. J.
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Pandemic influenza poses a constant threat to domestic and global public health with the potential of high morbidity and mortality. Pandemic virus-matched vaccines, though potentially highly efficacious, require a lead time before they can be mass-produced and distributed to the population. For this reason, mitigation strategies that provide protection ahead of widespread viral transmission are highly desirable. Here, we model the impact of a broadly cross-reactive seasonal influenza vaccine on pandemic influenza spread in the U.S. Our findings indicate that as the cross-protective efficacy of the seasonal vaccine against the pandemic variant increases, total hospitalizations are concomitantly reduced. For example, we find that even at typical seasonal vaccine uptake and modest vaccine efficacies against infection and hospitalization with the pandemic variant, total hospitalizations can be decreased by 30% or more across the scenarios considered. In addition, the hospitalization risk for recipients is at least 35% lower than the risk experienced by unvaccinated persons. These results demonstrate that a broadly protective influenza vaccine could serve as a critical component of pandemic preparedness strategies.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Pandemic influenza poses a constant threat to domestic and global public health with the potential of high morbidity and mortality. The study aimed to evaluate the potential of a broadly cross-reactive seasonal influenza vaccine to mitigate pandemic influenza spread.
Design and population
The study is a modeling analysis of pandemic influenza spread in the U.S., focusing on the impact of a broadly cross-reactive seasonal influenza vaccine. It considers various scenarios of vaccine uptake and efficacy against the pandemic variant.
Core methods
The research employed mathematical modeling to simulate the impact of a seasonal influenza vaccine with cross-reactive properties on pandemic influenza spread. The model incorporated typical seasonal vaccine uptake rates and assumed modest vaccine efficacies against infection and hospitalization with the pandemic variant.
Main results
The findings indicate that as the cross-protective efficacy of the seasonal vaccine against the pandemic variant increases, total hospitalizations are concomitantly reduced. For example, even at typical seasonal vaccine uptake and modest vaccine efficacies, total hospitalizations can be decreased by 30% or more across the scenarios considered.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret that a broadly protective influenza vaccine could serve as a critical component of pandemic preparedness strategies. This is a novel approach to pandemic mitigation by leveraging existing seasonal vaccine infrastructure.
Scientific and public-health significance
The results suggest that seasonal influenza vaccines with cross-reactive properties could have significant implications for pandemic preparedness, offering a potential tool to reduce hospitalizations and mitigate the impact of future outbreaks.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is based on modeling and does not involve empirical data, which limits the strength of the evidence. However, the findings are grounded in plausible assumptions about vaccine efficacy and uptake, supporting a moderate confidence level.

SNHG15 通过与 miR-153 和 RABL2A 的关联积极调节流感病毒感染。

SNHG15 Positively Regulates Influenza Virus Infection Through Its Association With miR-153 and RABL2A.
作者： Samuel Pushparaj, Kishore Vaddadi, Chaoqun Huang, Yurong Liang, Dao Xu, Gayan S Bamunuarachchi, Prince Jhandai, Keerthana Santhosh, Elizabeth Varghese, Lin Liu
摘要中文翻译摘要 流感病毒引起季节性流行,目前的 疫苗 并不能完全预防流感病毒。这项研究重点关注长链非编码 RNA (lncRNA),它们是不编码蛋白质但在各种生物过程中发挥关键作用的 RNA 分子。我们鉴定出 17 个被流感病毒改变的 lncRNA。其中,SNHG15被发现可增强流感病毒感染。使用 CRISPR 干扰和 RNA 干扰敲低 SNHG15 可减少流感病毒感染,而 SNHG15 过度表达可增加病毒复制。我们发现 miR-153-3p 是 SNHG15 的竞争性内源性 RNA (ceRNA),可作为抗流感 microRNA,其过度表达会抵消 SNHG15 的前病毒活性。RNA Pull-down 和质谱鉴定出 Rab 样蛋白 2A (RABL2A) 是 SNHG15 的相互作用伙伴。SNHG15 和 RABL2A 均促进流感病毒内化。此外,siRNA 介导的 RABL2A 敲低减少了 SNHG15 介导的病毒内化。总之,SNHG15 通过与 miR-153 和 RABL2A 的相互作用增强甲型流感病毒感染。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
流感病毒引起季节性流行,目前的 疫苗 并不能完全预防流感病毒。这项研究重点关注长链非编码 RNA (lncRNA),它们是不编码蛋白质但在各种生物过程中发挥关键作用的 RNA 分子。
设计与对象
该研究鉴定了 17 个被流感病毒改变的 lncRNA,重点关注 SNHG15 在增强流感病毒感染方面的作用。
核心方法
使用 CRISPR 干扰和 RNA 干扰对 SNHG15 进行敲低,并通过 RNA 下拉和质谱分析来鉴定 Rab 样蛋白 2A (RABL2A) 作为 SNHG15 的相互作用伙伴。
主要结果
SNHG15 的敲低减少了流感病毒的感染,而过度表达则增加了病毒的复制。miR-153-3p 是 SNHG15 的竞争性内源性 RNA,可作为抗流感 microRNA,其过度表达会抵消 SNHG15 的前病毒活性。SNHG15 和 RABL2A 均促进流感病毒内化。
解释与创新
作者解释说,SNHG15 通过与 miR-153 和 RABL2A 的相互作用增强甲型流感病毒感染,为流感病毒感染的分子机制提供了新的见解。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现可能为针对 lncRNA 或其相互作用伙伴的新治疗策略的开发提供信息,以对抗流感病毒感染。
局限与证据强度
该研究基于体外实验,由于缺乏体内验证和临床数据,证据强度中等。

SNHG15 Positively Regulates Influenza Virus Infection Through Its Association With miR-153 and RABL2A.

Authors: Samuel Pushparaj, Kishore Vaddadi, Chaoqun Huang, Yurong Liang, Dao Xu, Gayan S Bamunuarachchi, Prince Jhandai, Keerthana Santhosh, Elizabeth Varghese, Lin Liu
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Influenza viruses cause seasonal epidemics, and current vaccines do not fully protect against them. This study focuses on long non‐coding RNAs (lncRNAs), which are RNA molecules that do not code for proteins but play crucial roles in various biological processes. We identified 17 lncRNAs altered by the influenza virus. Among these, SNHG15 was found to enhance influenza virus infection. Knockdown of SNHG15 using CRISPR interference and RNA interference reduced influenza virus infection, while overexpression of SNHG15 increased virus replication. We found that miR‐153‐3p, a competitive endogenous RNA (ceRNA) for SNHG15, acts as an anti‐influenza microRNA, and its overexpression negated SNHG15's proviral activity. RNA pull‐down and mass spectrometry identified Rab‐like protein 2A (RABL2A) as an interacting partner of SNHG15. Both SNHG15 and RABL2A facilitated influenza virus internalization. Additionally, siRNA‐mediated knockdown of RABL2A reduced SNHG15‐mediated virus internalization. In conclusion, SNHG15 enhances influenza A virus infection through its interactions with miR‐153 and RABL2A.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Influenza viruses cause seasonal epidemics, and current vaccines do not fully protect against them. This study focuses on long non‐coding RNAs (lncRNAs), which are RNA molecules that do not code for proteins but play crucial roles in various biological processes.
Design and population
The study identified 17 lncRNAs altered by the influenza virus, with a focus on SNHG15's role in enhancing influenza virus infection.
Core methods
Knockdown of SNHG15 using CRISPR interference and RNA interference was performed, along with RNA pull‐down and mass spectrometry to identify Rab‐like protein 2A (RABL2A) as an interacting partner of SNHG15.
Main results
Knockdown of SNHG15 reduced influenza virus infection, while overexpression increased virus replication. miR‐153‐3p, a competitive endogenous RNA for SNHG15, acts as an anti‐influenza microRNA, and its overexpression negated SNHG15's proviral activity. Both SNHG15 and RABL2A facilitated influenza virus internalization.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret that SNHG15 enhances influenza A virus infection through its interactions with miR‐153 and RABL2A, providing novel insights into the molecular mechanisms of influenza virus infection.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings may inform the development of new therapeutic strategies targeting lncRNAs or their interacting partners to combat influenza virus infection.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is based on in vitro experiments, and the evidence strength is moderate due to the lack of in vivo validation and clinical data.

日本老年人感染流感 疫苗接种 的时间评估:VENUS 研究。

Assessment of Timing for Influenza Vaccination among Older Adults in Japan: The VENUS Study.
作者： Wataru Mimura, Chieko Ishiguro, Yosuke Shimizu, Junko Terada-Hirashima, Megumi Maeda, Futoshi Oda, Haruhisa Fukuda
摘要中文翻译目标:在日本,建议老年人在 10 月至 12 月中旬进行 季节性流感 疫苗接种;然而,由于免疫力下降和流行模式多变,疫苗接种 的最佳时机仍不清楚。在本研究中,我们旨在评估不同 疫苗接种 策略在减少老年人流感相关结果方面的有效性。方法:利用 VENUS 研究的数据,我们进行了一项目标试验模拟,包括四个流感季节(2016/2017、2017/2018、2018/2019 和 2019/2020)年龄≥65 岁的成年人。我们应用了克隆、审查和加权方法。每个人在 10 月初都被克隆并分配到七个 疫苗接种 策略之一:10 月期间无 疫苗接种、疫苗接种、10 月至 11 月、10 月至 12 月、11 月、11 月至 12 月或 12 月。结果包括流感、流感相关住院治疗和全因 死亡率。相对于无 发病率 的风险比 (RR) 和风险差 (RD) 在每个季节的 3 月底根据标准化累积 发病率 进行估计。结果:年度 队列研究 包括 131,178-138,438 人(中位年龄:76-78 岁;男性:40.1-41.0%)。流感 发病率 和 RR 随季节变化。延迟的 疫苗接种 与 2017/2018 和 2018/2019 年流感 发病率 最低水平相关,但与其他季节无关。与流感相关的住院治疗表现出与流感相似的季节性趋势。相比之下,早期的 疫苗接种 策略通常会导致所有季节的全因 死亡率 减少。2016/2017、2017/2018、2018/2019和2019/2020赛季的RR分别为0.80-0.93、0.80-0.95、0.82-1.00和0.81-0.96。死亡率 的 RD 大于流感相关结果的 RD。结论:每种计时策略的有效性因结果和季节而异。虽然延迟的 疫苗接种 在某些季节与较低的流感 发病率 相关,但早期的 疫苗接种 通常会减少全因 发病率。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
在日本,建议老年人在 10 月至 12 月中旬期间服用 季节性流感 疫苗接种;然而,由于免疫力下降和流行模式多变,疫苗接种 的最佳时机仍不清楚。在本研究中,我们旨在评估不同 疫苗接种 策略在减少老年人流感相关结果方面的有效性。
设计与对象
利用 VENUS 研究的数据,我们进行了一项目标试验模拟,包括四个流感季节(2016/2017、2017/2018、2018/2019 和 2019/2020)年龄≥65 岁的成年人。结果包括流感、流感相关住院治疗和全因 死亡率。
核心方法
我们应用了克隆、审查和加权方法。每个人在 10 月初都被克隆并分配到七个 疫苗接种 策略之一:10 月期间无 疫苗接种、疫苗接种、10 月至 11 月、10 月至 12 月、11 月、11 月至 12 月或 12 月。相对于无 发病率 的风险比 (RR) 和风险差 (RD) 在每个季节的 3 月底根据标准化累积 发病率 进行估计。
主要结果
年度 队列研究 包括 131,178-138,438 人(中位年龄:76-78 岁;男性:40.1-41.0%)。流感 发病率 和 RR 随季节变化。延迟的 疫苗接种 与 2017/2018 和 2018/2019 年流感 发病率 最低水平相关,但与其他季节无关。与流感相关的住院治疗表现出与流感相似的季节性趋势。相比之下,早期的 疫苗接种 策略通常会导致所有季节的全因 死亡率 减少。2016/2017、2017/2018、2018/2019和2019/2020赛季的RR分别为0.80-0.93、0.80-0.95、0.82-1.00和0.81-0.96。死亡率 的 RD 大于流感相关结果的 RD。
解释与创新
每种计时策略的有效性取决于结果和季节。虽然延迟的 疫苗接种 在某些季节与较低的流感 发病率 相关,但早期的 疫苗接种 通常会减少全因 发病率。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,疫苗接种 的时机应根据季节和结果进行调整,尽管对流感 发病率 的影响各不相同,但早期的 疫苗接种 可能会减少 死亡率。这可以为优化老年人流感 疫苗接种 策略的公共卫生建议提供参考。
局限与证据强度
该研究使用目标试验模拟,这可能会引入观察数据固有的局限性。然而,大样本量和跨多个季节的一致结果为研究结果提供了适度的信心。

Assessment of Timing for Influenza Vaccination among Older Adults in Japan: The VENUS Study.

Authors: Wataru Mimura, Chieko Ishiguro, Yosuke Shimizu, Junko Terada-Hirashima, Megumi Maeda, Futoshi Oda, Haruhisa Fukuda
Original abstract (English)
OBJECTIVES: In Japan, seasonal influenza vaccination is recommended for older adults between October and mid-December; however, the optimal vaccination timing remains unclear owing to waning immunity and variable epidemic patterns. In this study, we aimed to assess the effectiveness of different vaccination strategies in reducing influenza-related outcomes in older adults. METHODS: Using data from the VENUS Study, we conducted a target trial emulation including adults aged ≥65 years across four influenza seasons (2016/2017, 2017/2018, 2018/2019, and 2019/2020). We applied a cloning, censoring, and weighting approach. Each individual was cloned and assigned to one of seven vaccination strategies at the start of October: no vaccination, vaccination during October, October-November, October-December, November, November-December, or December. Outcomes included influenza, influenza-related hospitalization, and all-cause mortality. Risk ratios (RRs) and risk differences (RDs), relative to no vaccination, were estimated at the end of March for each season based on standardized cumulative incidence. RESULTS: Annual cohorts included 131,178-138,438 individuals (median age: 76-78 years; male sex: 40.1-41.0%). The influenza incidences and RRs varied by season. Delayed vaccination was associated with the lowest influenza incidence in 2017/2018 and 2018/2019, but not in the other seasons. Influenza-related hospitalizations showed seasonal trends similar to those of influenza. In contrast, early vaccination strategies generally led to reduced all-cause mortality across all seasons. The RRs were 0.80-0.93, 0.80-0.95, 0.82-1.00, and 0.81-0.96 in the 2016/2017, 2017/2018, 2018/2019, and 2019/2020 seasons, respectively. The RDs were larger for mortality than for the influenza-related outcomes. CONCLUSIONS: The effectiveness of each timing strategy varied depending on the outcome and season. Although delayed vaccination was associated with a lower influenza incidence in some seasons, early vaccination generally reduced all-cause mortality.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
In Japan, seasonal influenza vaccination is recommended for older adults between October and mid-December; however, the optimal vaccination timing remains unclear owing to waning immunity and variable epidemic patterns. In this study, we aimed to assess the effectiveness of different vaccination strategies in reducing influenza-related outcomes in older adults.
Design and population
Using data from the VENUS Study, we conducted a target trial emulation including adults aged ≥65 years across four influenza seasons (2016/2017, 2017/2018, 2018/2019, and 2019/2020). Outcomes included influenza, influenza-related hospitalization, and all-cause mortality.
Core methods
We applied a cloning, censoring, and weighting approach. Each individual was cloned and assigned to one of seven vaccination strategies at the start of October: no vaccination, vaccination during October, October-November, October-December, November, November-December, or December. Risk ratios (RRs) and risk differences (RDs), relative to no vaccination, were estimated at the end of March for each season based on standardized cumulative incidence.
Main results
Annual cohorts included 131,178-138,438 individuals (median age: 76-78 years; male sex: 40.1-41.0%). The influenza incidences and RRs varied by season. Delayed vaccination was associated with the lowest influenza incidence in 2017/2018 and 2018/2019, but not in the other seasons. Influenza-related hospitalizations showed seasonal trends similar to those of influenza. In contrast, early vaccination strategies generally led to reduced all-cause mortality across all seasons. The RRs were 0.80-0.93, 0.80-0.95, 0.82-1.00, and 0.81-0.96 in the 2016/2017, 2017/2018, 2018/2019, and 2019/2020 seasons, respectively. The RDs were larger for mortality than for the influenza-related outcomes.
Interpretation and novelty
The effectiveness of each timing strategy varied depending on the outcome and season. Although delayed vaccination was associated with a lower influenza incidence in some seasons, early vaccination generally reduced all-cause mortality.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that vaccination timing should be tailored to the season and outcome, with early vaccination potentially reducing mortality despite variable effects on influenza incidence. This could inform public health recommendations for optimizing influenza vaccination strategies in older adults.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study used target trial emulation, which may introduce limitations inherent to observational data. However, the large sample size and consistent results across multiple seasons provide moderate confidence in the findings.
