Update - acute respiratory infection activity in Tasmania Breadcrumb Home Topics Infectious Diseases Acute Respiratory Infection Activity Update - Acute Respiratory Infection Activity In Tasmania Listen Print Main navigation Infectious diseases Infectious diseases and prevention Infectious diseases guides and factsheets Acute respiratory infections Current acute respiratory infection activity in Tasmania Measles public exposure locations Rabies and Australian Bat Lyssavirus This page presents a snapshot of current acute respiratory infection activity in Tasmania. Current activity is presented for acute respiratory illness and specific notifiable viruses, these are COVID-19, influenza, and RSV. Acute respiratory illness is an overall indicator and may be due to symptoms of various infections including COVID-19, influenza, RSV, Rhinovirus and others. Activity may be presented as low, moderate or high. While monitoring by Public Health Services is continuous, this page is updated monthly during inter-seasonal periods. During increased seasonal influenza and RSV activity, this page is updated fortnightly.For detailed respiratory surveillance see RespTas report.Understanding current activity of acute respiratory infections will help you stay informed, follow activity level link to learn more about what steps you can take to reduce the risk to yourselves and others. Weekly update - acute respiratory infection activity in TasmaniaPublished: Friday 24 July 2026Data as at Sunday 19 July 2026ConditionCurrent activity Acute Respiratory IllnessLowLearn more about simple steps to follow when activity is LOWPathogen Current activityCOVID-19 LowInfluenzaLowRSVModerateWhat does this mean? As at Sunday 19 July 2026:Acute respiratory illness (ARI) activity remains low but is gradually increasing.COVID-19 activity remains low.Influenza activity remains low and below expected levels compared to recent years.Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) activity is moderate, continues to increase, and is consistent with seasonal activity. Notification rates continue to increase among children aged 0-4 years and adults aged 65 years and over.The next update will be published on Friday 7 August 2026.Published by the Health Intelligence team, Communicable Diseases Prevention Unit, Public Health Services. Sources: Tasmanian Notifiable Diseases Surveillance System; FluTracking, Hunter New England Local Health District, New South Wales Ministry of Health; DSPL — Diagnostic Services Pty Ltd; RHH — Royal Hobart Hospital Pathology.Other data sources: Notifiable disease data which includes notifiable acute respiratory infections, with breakdown by state and territory, is also published by the Commonwealth and updated daily at Dashboard · NINDSS Portal (health.gov.au)For detailed respiratory infection surveillance go to the RespTas Report pageHow you can help track ARI activity in TasmaniaYou can help Public Health to track acute respiratory illness by participating in FluTracking.FluTracking is an Australia-wide online health surveillance system operated by Hunter New England Health in NSW. It helps track the spread of COVID-19, flu and other respiratory illness. All Tasmanians are encouraged to participate, whether you’re healthy or unwell. All you need to do is complete a simple online survey each Monday, it takes less than 30 seconds. Moderna 的 mRNA Flu 疫苗 在后期试验中优于标准疫苗 - DD News

Moderna’s mRNA flu vaccine outperforms standard shot in late-stage trial - DD News
新闻简报Moderna 的 mRNA 流感 疫苗 在一项针对 40,000 多名 50 岁及以上成年人的后期国际试验中表现出卓越的功效,比传统流感疫苗高出 26.6%。疫苗 目前正在接受 FDA 审查,预计将于 8 月 5 日做出决定,如果获得批准,这将标志着美国首款使用 mRNA 技术的季节性流感疫苗。虽然注射部位疼痛和疲劳等副作用在 mRNA 注射中更为常见,但严重的副作用在各组之间是平衡的。FDA 最初拒绝了该申请,但在 Moderna 同意批准后对老年人进行一项额外研究后,FDA 改变了态度。欧盟、加拿大和澳大利亚也正在进行监管程序。
查看新闻五要素
时间
事件报道于 2026 年 7 月 5 日星期三;发布日期 2026 年 7 月 27 日;FDA 预计将于 2026 年 8 月 5 日做出决定。
地点与对象
涉及超过 40,000 名 50 岁及以上成年人的国际试验;美国、欧盟、加拿大和澳大利亚正在进行监管审查。
事件
已证实:Moderna 基于 mRNA 的季节性流感 疫苗 在一项后期国际试验中优于传统流感疫苗,其功效比 GSK 批准的标准剂量流感疫苗高 26.6%。
规模、影响与风险
40,000+ 50+成年人参与;疫苗 的功效比标准注射剂高 26.6%;mRNA 接受者中 2.2% 发生严重副作用,而标准剂量接受者中发生严重副作用的比例为 1.9%;如果获得批准,这将是美国第一个使用 mRNA 技术的季节性流感疫苗。
应对、状态与不确定性
疫苗 在最初拒绝后正在接受 FDA 审查;该机构改变了方针,接受了修改后的申请,并承诺在批准后对老年人进行额外的研究;欧盟、加拿大和澳大利亚也在进行监管审查;安全性与之前的后期试验一致;副作用大多为轻度至中度且短暂。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Moderna’s mRNA flu vaccine outperforms standard shot in late-stage trial - DD News

News Brief
Moderna's mRNA flu vaccine demonstrated superior efficacy in a late-stage international trial of over 40,000 adults aged 50 and older, outperforming conventional flu shots by 26.6%. The vaccine, currently under FDA review with a decision expected by August 5, would mark the first U.S. seasonal flu shot using mRNA technology if approved. While side effects like injection-site pain and fatigue were more common with the mRNA shot, severe adverse effects were balanced between groups. The FDA initially rejected the application but reversed course after Moderna agreed to conduct an additional study in older adults post-approval. Regulatory processes are also underway in the EU, Canada, and Australia.
Original source text (English)
Feedback | Monday, July 27, 2026 Skip Navigation DD News Menu Search Live Radio HI Top Stories National International Mann ki Baat Education Health Business & Economy Science & Tech Sports Environment Opinion Your browser does not support Javascript Tweets by DDNewslive Your browser does not support Javascript HomeTop StoriesModerna’s mRNA flu vaccine outperforms standard shot in late-stage trial 07/05/26 | 11:58 am | flu vaccine | late-stage trial | Moderna's mRNA flu vaccine | U.S. Food and Drug Administration Share Your browser does not support JavaScript Moderna’s mRNA flu vaccine outperforms standard shot in late-stage trial flu vaccine shot at Boston City Hall on January 7, 2026. (Photo by Suzanne Kreiter/The Boston Globe via Getty Images) Moderna’s mRNA-based seasonal flu vaccine worked better than conventional flu shots in a late-stage international trial of more than 40,000 adults aged 50 and older, researchers reported on Wednesday. The vaccine is currently under review by the U.S. Food and Drug Administration after the agency rejected the company’s initial application. A decision is expected by August 5. If approved, Moderna’s shot would be the first seasonal flu shot in the United States made with mRNA technology, a faster vaccine platform than conventional methods, but one Health Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr. has targeted for extra scrutiny. Kennedy, a long-time anti-vaccine activist, has been particularly critical of mRNA technology, canceling hundreds of millions in research funding and making claims not backed by scientific evidence. In the study, Moderna’s flu shot was 26.6% more effective than an approved standard-dose flu shot from GSK GSK.L, exceeding the study’s main goal of showing the shot was at least as good as existing vaccines. “These findings support the role of mRNA-1010 in improving influenza prevention,” Dr. Isabel Leroux-Roels of Ghent University and colleagues wrote in the study published in the New England Journal of Medicine. Side effects, including injection-site pain, fatigue, headache and muscle aches, were more common with the Moderna shot compared with the standard flu shot, but most were mild to moderate and short-lived. Severe side effects were balanced between the two groups, occurring in 2.2% of those who received the mRNA shot and 1.9% with the standard dose vaccine, researchers reported. Overall, safety was consistent with findings from prior late-stage trials, the study authors said. In February, the FDA rejected Moderna’s application, citing concerns about the standard dose flu shot used in the comparison group. The agency argued that the company should have tested its shot against a high-dose flu vaccine in people aged 65 and older, which is recommended in the United States because of its higher efficacy. After meeting with the company, the agency reversed course and accepted an amended application with the promise that Moderna would conduct an additional study in older adults once the vaccine is approved. Regulatory reviews for Moderna’s flu shot are also underway in the EU, Canada and Australia. The company’s combined mRNA flu and COVID-19 vaccine won European Commission approval in April. Event reported on Wednesday, July 5, 2026; publication date July 27, 2026; FDA decision expected by August 5, 2026.
Location and population
International trial involving more than 40,000 adults aged 50 and older; regulatory reviews underway in the U.S., EU, Canada, and Australia.

Confirmed: Moderna's mRNA-based seasonal flu vaccine outperformed conventional flu shots in a late-stage international trial, showing 26.6% higher efficacy than an approved standard-dose flu shot from GSK.
Scale, impact and risk
40,000+ adults aged 50+ participated; vaccine showed 26.6% higher efficacy than standard shot; severe side effects occurred in 2.2% of mRNA recipients vs. 1.9% of standard dose recipients; if approved, it would be the first U.S. seasonal flu shot using mRNA technology.

流行病和 季节性流感 疫苗 准备和响应:利用减轻 COVID-19 流行病努力中的经验教训

Pandemic and Seasonal Influenza Vaccine Preparedness and Response: Harnessing Lessons from the Efforts to Mitigate the COVID-19 Pandemic
新闻简报美国国家医学院成立了一个国际委员会,以评估 COVID-19 应对策略如何加强全球对大流行和 季节性流感 的准备,特别是在 疫苗 的开发方面。该倡议得到了美国卫生与公众服务全球事务办公室的支持,汇集了多学科专家来评估技术、政策和流程。四个并发委员会将重点关注 疫苗 的研发、分销和供应链、公共卫生干预措施以及国际协调。他们的研究结果将作为同行评审报告发布,以改进流感 疫苗 的设计、生产和 监测,目标是增强全球健康抵御未来流感威胁的能力。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-07-23。证据中未指定事件细节。
地点与对象
全球倡议涉及来自政府、学术界、工业界、民间社会和国际公共卫生组织的国际专家。
事件
美国国家医学院成立国际委员会,评估 COVID-19 应对能力对大流行和 季节性流感 准备和应对的影响,特别是 疫苗 开发的影响。
规模、影响与风险
没有报告具体的病例数、死亡或暴露情况。该倡议利用 COVID-19 的经验教训,重点关注流感 疫苗 的全球准备和应对策略。
应对、状态与不确定性
国际委员会将通过四个同时成立的特设委员会提供建议,发布有关疫苗设计、临床试验、生产、分销和监测的同行评审报告。这项工作正在进行中,目前没有报告未解决的信息。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Pandemic and Seasonal Influenza Vaccine Preparedness and Response: Harnessing Lessons from the Efforts to Mitigate the COVID-19 Pandemic

News Brief
The National Academy of Medicine has launched an International Committee to evaluate how COVID-19 response strategies can enhance global preparedness for pandemic and seasonal influenza, especially in vaccine development. This initiative, supported by the U.S. Health and Human Services Office of Global Affairs, brings together multidisciplinary experts to assess technologies, policies, and processes. Four concurrent committees will focus on vaccine research and development, distribution and supply chains, public health interventions, and international coordination. Their findings will be published as peer-reviewed reports to improve influenza vaccine design, production, and surveillance, with the goal of strengthening global health resilience against future influenza threats.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content Discover Events Engage About Donate Dropdown items My Academies Login Personal Library Account settings Logout Our Work Our Work Initiative Pandemic and Seasonal Influenza Vaccine Preparedness and Response: Harnessing Lessons from the Efforts to Mitigate the COVID-19 Pandemic Initiative Pandemic and Seasonal Influenza Vaccine Preparedness and Response: Harnessing Lessons from the Efforts to Mitigate the COVID-19 Pandemic The National Academy of Medicine has established an International Committee (IC) in coordination with the U.S. Health and Human Services Office of Global Affairs (OGA) to inform and facilitate its efforts to advance global influenza pandemic preparedness. The IC will provide OGA an iterative, interactive, multi-disciplinary, expert-informed process for assessing the global impact that capabilities, technologies, processes, and policies developed in response to COVID-19 could have on pandemic and seasonal influenza global preparedness and response, especially involving vaccine development. Work with us In progress On this page Statement of Task Work with us Contributors Contact Statement of Task The NAM will convene an International Committee of domestic and international experts from across sectors (e.g., government, academia, industry, civil society, international public health organizations) and a variety of disciplines to provide an iterative, interactive, multi-disciplinary, expert-informed process for assessing the global impact that capabilities, technologies, processes, and policies developed for COVID-19 could have on pandemic and seasonal influenza global preparedness and response, especially regarding vaccine development. The International Committee will provide recommendations developed by four concurrent National Academies ad hoc committees. The recommendations of these ad hoc committees will be released as four peer-reviewed reports on how to improve the global design, composition, clinical trials, production, scale-up, regulatory approval, distribution of influenza vaccines, and post-approval surveillance for adverse events.The four concurrent ad hoc committees will examine the emerging evidence on research and development for COVID-19 relevant to advancing seasonal and pandemic influenza global preparedness and response. The focus areas of the four consensus studies will be: Concurrent Consensus Study 1: Vaccine research and development, including platforms in discovery and manufacturing Concurrent Consensus Study 2: Distribution and supply chain; Concurrent Consensus Study 3: Public health interventions and countermeasures (NPIs, Diagnostics, and Treatment Strategies) Concurrent Consensus Study 4: International coordination, innovative partnerships, and sustainable financing for Influenza preparedness and response Work with Us Looking for independent, nonpartisan guidance from the nation’s top subject-matter experts? We stand ready to help potential sponsors across a variety of sectors shape research and policy agendas, regulation, investments, operations, state and local initiatives, and more. COVID-19 现在只是另一种季节性流感吗?专家说不 - 今日商业

Is COVID-19 just another seasonal flu now? Experts say no - Business Today
新闻简报COVID-19 has entered an endemic phase in India but should not be dismissed as just another seasonal flu, experts warn. While widespread immunity has reduced severity for most healthy individuals, the virus continues to hospitalize and kill vulnerable populations, including older adults, those with comorbidities, and immunocompromised individuals. New variants like NB.1.8.1 and JN.1 descendants show high transmission but no increased severity. India has recorded over 4.5 crore cases and 5.3 lakh deaths since 2020, with recent localised clusters prompting genomic surveillance. Authorities emphasize continued vigilance, protection of high-risk groups, and maintaining public health preparedness as the virus evolves and circulates.
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026 年 7 月 23 日。事件背景:印度部分地区最近发现新的 COVID-19 病例和死亡病例。
地点与对象
印度,特别提到安得拉邦,那里的局部簇促进了基因组测序。受影响人群:老年人、有基础疾病、免疫力低下等弱势群体。
事件
COVID-19 正处于流行阶段,但不应与 季节性流感 等同。专家证实,它继续导致住院和死亡,特别是在高危人群中。该病毒正在传播,其新变种(NB.1.8.1 和 JN.1 后代)显示出较高的 传播,但严重性并未增加。
规模、影响与风险
India has recorded over 4.5 crore confirmed COVID-19 cases and more than 5.3 lakh deaths since 2020. Recent sporadic infections and occasional deaths, including localised clusters, indicate ongoing circulation. High-risk groups remain vulnerable to severe disease, hospitalisation, and ICU care.
应对、状态与不确定性
印度已从应急响应转向长期管理,重点保护弱势群体、加强基因组测序、保持公共卫生准备。继续强调 监测、早期症状识别、及时检测以及针对免疫功能低下个体的加强 疫苗接种。疫情已经结束,但威胁依然存在。

Is COVID-19 just another seasonal flu now? Experts say no - Business Today

News Brief
COVID-19 has entered an endemic phase in India but should not be dismissed as just another seasonal flu, experts warn. While widespread immunity has reduced severity for most healthy individuals, the virus continues to hospitalize and kill vulnerable populations, including older adults, those with comorbidities, and immunocompromised individuals. New variants like NB.1.8.1 and JN.1 descendants show high transmission but no increased severity. India has recorded over 4.5 crore cases and 5.3 lakh deaths since 2020, with recent localised clusters prompting genomic surveillance. Authorities emphasize continued vigilance, protection of high-risk groups, and maintaining public health preparedness as the virus evolves and circulates.
Original source text (English)
July 28, 2026 01:12 ISTBusiness TodayBT BazaarIndia TodayNortheastIndia Today GamingCosmopolitanHarper's BazaarBrides TodayIshq FMAaj TakGNTTVKisan TakLallantopMalyalamBanglaSports TakCrime TakAajtak CampusAstro takSubscribeHomeMarketsStocks News IPO Corner Indices Stocks List MagazineCover Story Editor's Note Deep Dive Interview BT TVMarket Today Easynomics Drive Today BT Explainer NewBT BillionairesGold & Silver RateIndiaTechArtificial Intelligence Tech News Startups Unbox - Review BT AutoIndustryBanking IT Energy Commodities Pharma Real Estate Telecom Money TodayMutual Funds Tax Investment Insurance Tools & Calculator eventsSpecial NRICareer & JobsUS NewsIn PicsWeatherPetrol PricesDiesel PricesPMS TodayBT ReelsSearchAdvertisement HomeMarketBT TVReelsMenuBusiness NewsindustrypharmaIs COVID-19 just another seasonal flu now? Experts say noIs COVID-19 just another seasonal flu now? Experts say noExperts say COVID-19 should not be dismissed as just another seasonal flu. While widespread immunity has reduced the severity of infection, the virus continues to hospitalise and kill vulnerable people.Join Our WhatsApp ChannelNeetu Chandra SharmaUpdated Jul 23, 2026 2:44 PM ISTDoctors say COVID-19 and influenza now share many common symptoms, including fever, cough, sore throat, fatigue and body ache. The recent detection of fresh COVID-19 cases and deaths in parts of India has once again raised questions about whether the virus should now be treated as just another seasonal respiratory infection. While COVID-19 has entered an endemic phase and is no longer causing the devastating waves seen during the pandemic, doctors say it continues to pose a serious threat to older adults, people with underlying medical conditions and those with weakened immunity.Advertisement Immunity has changed the disease, not the virus According to Dr Rajni Kant, Indian Council of Medical Research (ICMR) Chair for Disease Elimination and former Director of ICMR-RMRC, Gorakhpur, COVID-19 has become less severe for most healthy individuals, but that does not mean the disease has become harmless. “COVID-19 is now in the endemic phase, but it should not be equated with seasonal influenza. Widespread vaccination and previous exposure to the virus have helped build immunity in the population, which is why the infection is generally less severe in healthy individuals. However, people with comorbidities, the elderly and other high-risk groups remain vulnerable to severe disease. COVID-19 continues to cause hospitalisations and deaths, so it should not be taken lightly. Those at higher risk should continue to take appropriate precautions and seek medical attention promptly if they develop symptoms,” he said.Advertisement Doctors say COVID-19 and influenza now share many common symptoms, including fever, cough, sore throat, fatigue and body ache. But similar symptoms do not mean the two infections carry the same risk. Unlike influenza, SARS-CoV-2 continues to evolve, giving rise to new variants that can spread even among people with existing immunity. New variants keep COVID-19 in circulation The variants currently circulating, including NB.1.8.1 and other descendants of the JN.1 lineage, appear to spread more easily, although there is no evidence so far that they cause more severe disease. “The currently circulating COVID-19 variants, including NB.1.8.1 and other JN.1 descendants, have a high transmission rate but are not associated with increased severity. Although symptoms overlap with those of seasonal infections, infections continue because of waning immunity and ongoing viral evolution, similar to other respiratory viruses,” said Dr Neha Rastogi, Senior Consultant, Infectious Diseases, Fortis Hospital, Gurugram.Advertisement She said older adults, immunocompromised individuals and people with significant comorbidities remain at higher risk of developing severe illness. “In view of the vulnerability of these groups, prompt alertness, appropriate screening and continued surveillance remain essential. Continued genomic surveillance and protection of vulnerable populations should remain priorities. Early identification of symptoms, timely testing and appropriate preventive measures, including booster vaccination for immunocompromised individuals, continue to be important,” she said. The pandemic is over, but the threat remains According to Dr Randeep Guleria, Chairman, Institute of Internal Medicine, Respiratory and Sleep Medicine at Medanta Hospital, COVID-19 is not causing the level of illness seen during the earlier waves, but it has not become just another seasonal infection. “COVID-19 is no longer as severe as it was during the earlier waves, largely because of widespread immunity from vaccination and prior infections. However, it would be a mistake to dismiss it as just another seasonal flu. We are still seeing patients with COVID-19 requiring hospitalisation, including ICU care, particularly among older adults, those with weakened immunity and people with underlying illnesses. For these high-risk groups, the virus can still lead to serious complications, so vigilance and timely medical care remain important,” he said.Advertisement India has moved from emergency response to long-term management of COVID-19, with greater emphasis on protecting vulnerable populations, strengthening genomic sequencing and maintaining public health preparedness. The virus continues to circulate. Since the first cases were reported in 2020, India has recorded more than 4.5 crore confirmed COVID-19 cases and over 5.3 lakh deaths, according to official government data. In recent months, the country has reported sporadic infections and occasional deaths, including localised clusters in Andhra Pradesh that prompted genomic sequencing after newer variants were detected. With over two decades of experience in top Indian newsrooms—including Mint, Hindustan Times, the India Today Group, and Zee Media—she has consistently reported on the intersection of public health policy, corporate healthcare, and the pharmaceutical industry. Her journalism spans policy deep dives, industry trends, regulatory shifts, and corporate strategy across India’s thriving health ecosystem. 班加罗尔流感病例突然激增 - 班加罗尔镜报

Bengaluru records sudden spike in influenza cases - Bangalore Mirror
新闻简报班加罗尔的 季节性流感 病例突然激增,尤其是儿童病例,医生报告 H1N1 和 H3N2 感染有所增加。一家城市医院已记录约 60 例病例,平均每天 3-5 例。症状包括高烧、嗜睡和轻度呼吸系统问题,更严重的症状包括剧烈头痛和身体疼痛。激增的原因是学校重新开放、雨季和免疫力下降。虽然大多数儿童无需住院即可康复,但也可能因肺炎等并发症而入院。医生强调每年的疫苗接种作为主要的预防措施,特别是对于孕妇来说,以保护新生儿。与典型的季节性模式相比,今年的 暴发 更加引人注目。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026年7月22日。活动期间:2026年7月22日当周,每日报告病例。
地点与对象
地点:印度班加罗尔。受影响人群:儿童,特别是在门诊和急诊科、学校等有密切接触环境的儿童。
事件
据市医院医生报告,确认 季节性流感 暴发 的 H1N1 和 H3N2 感染明显增加。
规模、影响与风险
病例:市一医院报告约60例,平均每天3-5例。症状包括突然高烧、嗜睡、轻微感冒/咳嗽,以及严重头痛、身体疼痛和疲劳等严重症状。风险因素包括学校重新开放、雨季和免疫力下降。
应对、状态与不确定性
医生指出,这种激增是季节性的,但今年更为明显。预防措施包括每年疫苗接种,特别是针对孕妇。入院通常是由于肺炎或脱水等并发症。除了 疫苗接种 建议之外,没有提及任何具体的公共卫生干预措施。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bengaluru records sudden spike in influenza cases - Bangalore Mirror

News Brief
Bengaluru is experiencing a sudden spike in seasonal influenza cases, particularly among children, with doctors reporting an increase in H1N1 and H3N2 infections. At one city hospital, around 60 cases have been recorded, averaging 3-5 daily. Symptoms include high-grade fever, lethargy, and mild respiratory issues, with more severe features like intense headache and body pain. The surge is attributed to school reopenings, the rainy season, and waning immunity. While most children recover without hospitalization, admissions may occur due to complications like pneumonia. Doctors emphasize annual vaccination as the primary preventive measure, especially for pregnant women, to protect newborns. The outbreak is noted to be more noticeable this year compared to typical seasonal patterns.
Original source text (English)
WELCOME,My TimesLOGOUTSIGN INTue ,Jul 28, 2026Mumbai Mirror|Pune MirrorHomeBangaloreCover StoryCrimeCivicOtherElectionsEntertainmentBollywoodHollywoodReviewsLoungeSouth MasalaVideossectionhomelistNewsSportsEntertainmentPhotosEntertainmentNewsSportsSportsCricketFootballTennisOthersNewsStateIndiaWorldBusinessOpinionAsk the SexpertSunday ReadYouCode 560OthersViewsFoodTeen SpiritBlogsLoksabha ElectionsPhotosVideosScheduleCampaign TrackerModi MeterCoronavirusAll CoronavirusCover StoryCrimeCivicOtherElectionsNewsPhotosVideosBangaloreCover storyCrimeCivicOthersEntertainmentBollywoodHollywoodReviewsLoungeSouth MasalaVideosNewsSportsEntertainmentPhotosEntertainmentNewsSportsSportsCricketFootballTennisOthersNewsStateIndiaWorldBusinessOpinionAsk the SexpertSunday ReadYouCode 560OthersViewsFoodTeen SpiritBlogsOthersLetters to the EditorSci-TechLeisureTrending TodayLit.Mus 2014Home/Bangalore/Other/Bengaluru records sudden spike in influenza casesBengaluru records sudden spike in influenza casesByIffath FathimaIffath Fathima / Updated: Jul 22, 2026, 06:00 ISTFacebookTwitterLinkedinEmailShareFacebookTwitterLinkedinEmailPrint this articleReduce font sizeIncrease font sizeChildren may be more susceptible to infections due to close interactions at school (File photo)Doctors report noticeable seasonal upswing in influenza, especially among children, with hospitals recording cases marked by sudden fever along with mild respiratory symptomsBengaluru is witnessing a rise in seasonal influenza cases, with many children being the most affected. Doctors say they are seeing an increase in H1N1 and H3N2 infections this week, with around 60 cases reported at one city hospital unit, averaging three to five cases a day. The infections are largely present with sudden high-grade fever, lethargy and mild cold or cough symptoms.Paediatricians across the city say the current increase, while seasonal, is more noticeable in outpatient and emergency departments as schools remain open and children spend more time in close-contact settings.Dr Harini Sreedharan, Consultant - Paediatrician & Neonatologist, Motherhood Hospitals, Sarjapur, said, “There are periodic and seasonal outbreaks of the H3N2 and H1N1. There are many factors which drive these seasonal outbreaks and seasonal epidemics. This could be due to the changes in the vaccination of the population status or mutations of the viruses which happen at a cyclical rate.”Echoing the trend, Dr Radhakrishna Hegde S, HOD and Lead Consultant - Paediatrics, Aster Whitefield Hospital, said, “The number of influenza cases including those due to H1N1 and H3N2 infections is increasing significantly. While the number of influenza patients in this period of the year is not surprising, the current surge is noteworthy in regards to the paediatric outpatient departments and emergency departments.”He added, “There may be several explanations for that. First, the viruses become more easily transmitted if schools operate, as children are likely to have close interaction thus making them efficient vectors of viral infections. Second, the rainy season adds another risk factor as people tend to stay indoors in poorly ventilated rooms. Third, immunity to influenza viruses fades away over time while changing each year, and therefore, some children do not have immunity to some strains despite having been sick before. Parents prefer not to vaccinate their children against influenza each year.”Doctors said influenza often resembles a common viral fever but typically causes more severe symptoms. Dr Sreedharan said, “The influenza that is the H1N1 or H3N2 may be indistinguishable from the common viral fever, but they can have more general features in terms of intense headache, intense throat pain, body pain, and, general feeling of fatigue and lethargy.”Dr Hegde said, “It can be a little hard to tell influenza from other viral illnesses just from symptoms, because a lot of respiratory viruses look pretty similar in the beginning. Still influenza tends to kick off quite fast, with a real sudden onset, high-grade fever, chills, intense body ache, headache, extreme tiredness, sore throat and a cough.”Dr Hegde noted that while some children with influenza are admitted to hospitals, most recover without requiring hospitalisation. “The admissions are typically because of pneumonia, dehydration, breathing difficulty or the situation gets worse with a pre-existing medical condition,” he said.On prevention, Dr Sreedharan said, “Ideal preventive measure is to take the vaccination and to make sure that you take it yearly. And there is a particular month where the vaccination or the new strain comes out so make sure you’re up to date on that. All pregnant women should be vaccinated as their vaccines are passed across the placenta and help in protection of the newborn till six months of age.”Dr Hegde added, “Vaccination against influenza is the most efficient prevention measure, and it should be done annually since vaccines are updated according to the circulating strains.” GALLERIES View more photosAero India 2021: 13th edition of air show begins todayPedestrians Only!PM lays foundation stone of new Parliament buildingMost PopularMost ReadMost commentedFootpath joy fades as encroachers retur... 疫苗 指南正在迅速变化——以下是您需要了解的内容 - Pharmacy Times

Vaccine Guidance Is Rapidly Changing—Here's What You Need to Know - Pharmacy Times
新闻简报美国的 疫苗 指南正在迅速发展,流感、COVID-19 和 RSV 的免疫接种计划发生了重大变化。一名联邦法官于 2026 年 3 月阻止了 CDC 的一些日程变更,恢复了更广泛的访问范围。药剂师在 疫苗 的给药和患者教育方面变得越来越重要,特别是在流感 疫苗接种 发病率下降的情况下。对于 2026-2027 年季节,FDA 已为 疫苗 选择了特定的流感毒株,但 COVID-19 毒株仍不确定。RSV 疫苗 资格已扩大到包括年轻的高危成年人。研究继续确定未来的 RSV 疫苗 需求,有证据表明药剂师主导的干预措施显着提高了多个 疫苗 中 疫苗接种 的吸收。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-07-27。赛事参考包括 2026 年 3 月和即将到来的 2026-2027 赛季的变化。
地点与对象
一般人群,特别关注成人、儿科患者、老年人 (65 岁以上)、免疫功能低下的个体以及严重 RSV 疾病风险较高的人群。
事件
免疫接种计划指南发生变化,包括流感、COVID-19 和 RSV 疫苗,其中药剂师在管理和教育中发挥着关键作用。联邦法官于 2026 年 3 月阻止了 CDC 的一些时间表变更。
规模、影响与风险
成人和儿童患者的流感 疫苗 发病率下降。2026-2027 年季节的 COVID-19 疫苗 不确定性。RSV 疫苗 扩展到 18-49 岁,风险增加。列出了严重 RSV 疾病的危险因素。
应对、状态与不确定性
提供教育和 疫苗 管理的药剂师。国家政策可能与 CDC 指导不同。未来 RSV 疫苗 剂量的研究正在进行中。系统评价显示,药剂师主导的干预措施对 疫苗 的吸收率更高。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Vaccine Guidance Is Rapidly Changing—Here's What You Need to Know - Pharmacy Times

News Brief
Vaccine guidance in the US is rapidly evolving, with significant changes to immunization schedules for influenza, COVID-19, and RSV. A federal judge blocked some CDC schedule changes in March 2026, restoring broader access. Pharmacists are increasingly vital in administering vaccines and educating patients, especially as influenza vaccination rates decline. For the 2026-2027 season, the FDA has selected specific influenza strains for vaccines, though COVID-19 strains remain uncertain. RSV vaccine eligibility has expanded to include younger at-risk adults. Research continues to determine future RSV vaccine needs, with evidence showing pharmacist-led interventions significantly boost vaccination uptake across multiple vaccines.
Original source text (English)
Vaccine guidance is continuously changing, and misinformation can make patients more vulnerable to vaccine-preventable diseases.1 This is evident with ongoing measles outbreaks throughout the US due to lower measles, mumps, and rubella (MMR) immunization rates.2 Pharmacists can administer vaccines and provide education about the importance of immunizations during medication therapy management (MTM) consults. Vaccine Updates and MTM The US Department of Health and Human Services (HHS) and the CDC Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices (ACIP) made drastic changes to the vaccine schedule over the past year.3 This included removing the universal hepatitis B vaccine recommendation for newborns and placing certain childhood vaccines under shared clinical decision-making (SCDM).3 COVID-19 vaccine recommendations were also moved under SCDM. However, a federal judge blocked these immunization schedule changes in March 2026, restoring greater access.3,4 Pharmacists should refer to their state policies regarding vaccines because some are now following organizations such as the American Academy of Pediatrics and the American Academy of Family Physicians instead of the CDC for immunization guidance.4 As of this writing, the strain for the upcoming 2026-2027 season has not been selected for COVID-19 vaccines, making an updated vaccine uncertain. Pharmacists can identify patients who are eligible for additional COVID-19 vaccines, such as those 65 years and older and immunocompromised individuals, who can receive 2 COVID-19 vaccines from the 2025-2026 season, spaced 6 months apart.5 Influenza vaccine rates for adults and pediatric patients have declined.6 Pharmacists can educate patients about the importance of receiving an annual influenza vaccine. The FDA selected the following influenza strains for the egg-based influenza vaccines for the 2026-2027 season7: An A/Missouri/11/2025 (H1N1)pdm09–like virus An A/Darwin/1454/2025 (H3N2)–like virus A B/Tokyo/ElS13-175/2025 (B/Victoria lineage)–like virus The cell- or recombinant-based influenza vaccines will contain the following strains7: An A/Missouri/11/2025 (H1N1)pdm09–like virus An A/Darwin/1415/2025 (H3N2)–like virus A B/Pennsylvania/14/2025 (B/Victoria lineage)–like virus In March 2026, the FDA expanded the age range for approval of the respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) vaccine Arexvy (GSK).8 It is now approved to include patients aged 18 to 49 years who are at increased risk of lower respiratory tract disease caused by RSV.8 This now falls in line with the other RSV vaccines, Abrysvo (Pfizer) and mResvia (Moderna). The CDC recommends a single dose of an RSV vaccine for individuals 75 years and older and adults 50 to 74 years at increased risk of severe RSV illness.9 The following factors can increase the risk of severe RSV illness, although health care teams can identify other factors as appropriate9: Chronic cardiovascular disease Chronic lung or respiratory disease End-stage renal disease Diabetes Neurologic or neuromuscular conditions Chronic liver disease Chronic hematologic conditions Severe obesity Moderate or severe immunocompromised status Residing in a nursing home Pharmacists play a vital role in RSV vaccine uptake. If patients have not yet received an RSV vaccine, pharmacists can recommend 1 dose between August and October, before RSV season typically starts. Because the RSV vaccine is not an annual immunization, research is underway to determine whether additional doses are needed in the future.9 One systematic review and meta-analysis evaluated 13 studies on pharmacist-led interventions for certain recommended vaccines in patients 60 years and older.10 The vaccines included those for COVID-19, herpes zoster, pneumococcal, influenza, and RSV. The study results indicated higher vaccine uptake with pharmacist-led immunization services. Results from studies involving pharmacists as educators, facilitators, and advocates showed the highest vaccine uptake. Additionally, the herpes zoster and pneumococcal vaccines had the highest uptake with pharmacist-led immunization services. These findings highlight the importance of pharmacists as part of the health care team for encouraging vaccination. MTM Spotlight In an interview with Pharmacy Times, Ellen Jones, PharmD, MA, BCACP, associate professor of pharmacy practice at the Harding University College of Pharmacy in Searcy, Arkansas, discussed the pharmacist’s role in immunizations. Jones teaches the American Pharmacists Association (APhA) Immunization Certificate program to first-year pharmacy students. “I provide a comprehensive review of how to determine which vaccines an individual needs by interpreting the CDC vaccine schedule. The APhA updates its program regularly to include any relevant updates,” Jones said. She also discussed the new recommendation that all influenza vaccines be thimerosal-free for the 2025-2026 flu season. “My practice site is a small direct primary care clinic, and we refer our patients to their local community pharmacy for vaccines. The most common ones would be for an annual influenza vaccine and tetanus, diphtheria, and pertussis boosters, particularly for patients who present with injuries with a high risk for tetanus,” she said. “Another one we recommend is the pneumococcal vaccine. I’m thankful for the CDC’s PneumoRecs VaxAdvisor to help navigate the different pneumococcal vaccine options.” About the Author Jennifer Gershman, PharmD, CPh, PACS, is a drug information pharmacist and Pharmacy Times contributor who resides in South Florida. 沙巴纳·阿兹米 (Shabana Azmi) 感染猪流感,建议隔离:关于 H1N1 您需要了解的信息 - Times Now

Shabana Azmi Down With Swine Flu, Advised Isolation: What You Need to Know About H1N1 - Times Now
新闻简报印度资深演员沙巴纳·阿兹米 (Shabana Azmi) 被诊断患有猪流感(甲型 H1N1 流感),并在出现 102°F 的高烧后建议隔离五天。这名 75 岁的老人患有哮喘,无法参加孟买的学生抗议活动,目前正在医疗监督下康复。她的案例凸显了 H1N1 流感的持续传播,尤其影响老年人和患有哮喘等慢性病的人。健康专家强调早期 治疗、隔离和 疫苗接种 等预防措施和手部卫生的重要性。大多数病例可通过支持性护理得到缓解,但症状严重或高危人群则需要医疗护理。

Shabana Azmi Down With Swine Flu, Advised Isolation: What You Need to Know About H1N1 - Times Now

News Brief
Veteran Indian actor Shabana Azmi has been diagnosed with swine flu (Influenza A H1N1) and advised to isolate for five days after developing a high fever of 102°F. The 75-year-old, who has asthma, was unable to attend a student protest in Mumbai and is currently recovering under medical supervision. Her case highlights the ongoing circulation of H1N1, particularly affecting older adults and those with chronic conditions like asthma. Health experts stress the importance of early treatment, isolation, and preventive measures such as vaccination and hand hygiene. Most cases resolve with supportive care, but medical attention is needed for severe symptoms or high-risk individuals.

Shabana Azmi Diagnosed With Swine Flu (Pics: Instagram/iStock)follow usfollow usShabana Azmi has been diagnosed with swine flu (Influenza A H1N1) and has been advised complete bed rest and five days of isolation by doctors. The 75-year-old actor was unable to attend a student protest in Mumbai after developing a high fever of 102°F and is currently recovering under medical supervision. According to her team, Azmi was expected to participate in a demonstration after spending several days supporting student protests in Delhi. However, her illness forced her to suspend all public appearances while she recovers from the viral infection. Her diagnosis serves as a reminder that H1N1 influenza continues to circulate seasonally and can cause serious illness, particularly in older adults and people with underlying health conditions.Breaking It DownWhat are common complications of swine flu?How does swine flu spread?What preventive measures can be taken against swine flu?Created with AI.Also read: Shabana Azmi Health Update: Actress Advised Isolation After Swine Flu Diagnosis - Read StatementWhat is swine flu or H1N1? Swine flu is a contagious respiratory infection caused by the Influenza A (H1N1) virus. Although it first gained worldwide attention during the 2009 pandemic, the virus now circulates as one of the seasonal influenza strains. The infection spreads through: Respiratory droplets released while coughing or sneezing Close contact with an infected person Touching contaminated surfaces and then touching the eyes, nose, or mouth The virus is highly contagious during the first few days of illness, making isolation important to reduce transmission.Common signs and symptoms of H1N1 Swine flu symptoms often resemble those of seasonal influenza and usually appear suddenly. Common signs include: High fever Persistent cough Sore throat Runny or blocked nose Body aches Headache Extreme fatigue Chills Loss of appetite Some people may also experience nausea, vomiting or diarrhoea, especially children. Who is at higher risk? While many healthy people recover within a week, certain groups are more likely to develop severe complications. These include adults aged 65 years and older, young children, pregnant women, people with asthma or chronic lung disease, individuals with diabetes, patients with heart disease and those with weakened immune systems. Notably, Shabana Azmi has previously shared that she has asthma, a condition that can increase the risk of influenza-related breathing complications.Why is isolation important? Doctors have advised Azmi to remain in isolation for five days, a common recommendation to reduce the spread of influenza to others. People with H1N1 are generally advised to: Stay home until fever subsides Wear a mask if contact with others is unavoidable Wash hands frequently Cover coughs and sneezes Avoid crowded public places while infectious Adequate rest, hydration, and prescribed antiviral medications, when indicated, can help speed recovery and reduce the risk of complications. When should you seek medical care? Most cases improve with supportive care. However, immediate medical attention is needed if symptoms include difficulty breathing, persistent chest pain, bluish lips or face, confusion, severe dehydration, high fever that does not improve, and worsening symptoms after initial recovery. Early treatment is particularly important for people in high-risk groups. How can you prevent swine flu? Health experts recommend several simple measures to reduce the risk of H1N1 infection: Get the annual seasonal flu vaccine Wash hands regularly with soap and water Avoid close contact with sick individuals Cover your mouth and nose while coughing or sneezing Stay home when unwell Maintain a healthy lifestyle to support immunity Shabana's swine flu diagnosis highlights that H1N1 remains an important seasonal health concern. Although most people recover completely, older adults and individuals with chronic conditions such as asthma should seek medical advice promptly if flu-like symptoms develop. 设计出具有粘性持久力的流感抗体

Engineering an antibody against flu with sticky staying power
新闻简报俄亥俄州立大学的科学家和合作机构开发了一种新型单克隆抗体,旨在对抗甲型流感病毒。这种基于 IgM 的抗体通过鼻喷雾剂输送,对小鼠具有显着的保护作用,使其免受致死剂量的 H1N1 和 H3N2 毒株的侵害。与传统抗体不同,这种工程分子将 IgM 的广泛结合能力与成熟抗体的精确靶向结合起来,降低了病毒突变和逃逸的能力。鼻喷雾剂配方使抗体能够粘附在肺内膜上,为小鼠提供长达 7 天的持续保护。这项研究发表在《自然通讯》上,为未来针对季节性流感和潜在大流行毒株(包括禽流感)的治疗提供了一个有前途的平台,尽管人类应用仍处于临床前阶段。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026 年 7 月 24 日。活动日期:2025 年 5 月 8 日(研究结果)。
地点与对象
美国(俄亥俄州立大学、休斯顿大学、德克萨斯大学休斯顿健康科学中心)。研究对象:小鼠。
事件
科学家设计了一种通过鼻喷雾剂输送的单克隆抗体(基于 IgM),可保护小鼠免受致命剂量的甲型流感(H1N1 和 H3N2 毒株)的侵害。该研究于 2025 年 4 月 29 日发表在《自然通讯》上。
规模、影响与风险
接受抗体的小鼠没有表现出 H3N2 疾病,大多数在 H1N1 流感中幸存下来,症状也有所恢复。该抗体在小鼠粘膜表面持续有效 7 天,表明对人类具有更持久保护的潜力。没有人类病例或死亡报告。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究处于临床前阶段(小鼠研究)。该抗体是一种概念验证,具有未来治疗季节性流感和可能的大流行毒株(包括禽流感(H5))的潜力。目前没有人类应用或官方回应的报道。来源限制:已审查完整的原始文章正文。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Engineering an antibody against flu with sticky staying power

News Brief
Scientists at Ohio State University and collaborating institutions have developed a novel monoclonal antibody engineered to combat influenza A viruses. The IgM-based antibody, delivered via nasal spray, demonstrated significant protection in mice against lethal doses of H1N1 and H3N2 strains. Unlike traditional antibodies, this engineered molecule combines the broad binding capacity of IgM with the precise targeting of mature antibodies, reducing the virus's ability to mutate and escape. The nasal spray formulation allows the antibody to adhere to lung lining, providing sustained protection for up to seven days in mice. Published in Nature Communications, this research offers a promising platform for future therapies against seasonal flu and potentially pandemic strains, including bird flu, though human applications remain in the preclinical stage.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content Your source for the latest research news Follow: Facebook X/Twitter Subscribe: RSS Feeds Newsletter New! Sign up for our free email newsletter. Science News from research organizations Engineering an antibody against flu with sticky staying power Aerosol treatment adheres to lung lining, blocks infection in mice Date: May 8, 2025 Source: Ohio State University Summary: Scientists have engineered a monoclonal antibody that can protect mice from a lethal dose of influenza A, a new study shows. The new molecule combines the specificity of a mature flu fighter with the broad binding capacity of a more general immune system defender. Share: Facebook Twitter Pinterest LinkedIN Email FULL STORY Scientists have engineered a monoclonal antibody that can protect mice from a lethal dose of influenza A, a new study shows. The new molecule combines the specificity of a mature flu fighter with the broad binding capacity of a more general immune system defender. The protective effect was enhanced by delivering the antibody in a nasal spray that disperses these molecules throughout the respiratory tract, where they stick to the slippery mucus lining to lie in wait for invading viral particles. The engineered molecule is based on the IgM version of an immunoglobulin antibody, the generic first line of antibody defense against infection -- but with an added structural feature that attaches to a very specific location on the influenza A surface to neutralize the virus. "It's a better platform with a better antibody. If we can prepare the respiratory environment with this enhanced engineered molecule, it can capture and intercept the virus in an early stage," said co-lead author Kai Xu, assistant professor of veterinary biosciences at The Ohio State University. "This would be a therapy that not only can be used for prevention of seasonal flu -- it potentially could also be used against pandemic strains in the future." The study was published April 29 in Nature Communications. Influenza A viruses are divided into subtypes based on characteristics of two major surface proteins, hemagglutinin (HA) and neuraminidase (NA) -- as in, for example, the strain annually circulating in people, H1N1, and a less common H3N2 virus that co-circulates during flu season. Previous work has shown that monoclonal IgG antibodies engineered to target the influenza A HA protein lose potency as the virus mutates its way out of their crosshairs. These antibodies, mimicking mature natural flu-fighters of the adaptive immune system, attach to a precise section of the surface protein to disable the virus's ability to bind to a host cell receptor and enter the cell to make copies of itself. But once viruses figure out where they're being targeted by antibodies, they mutate those sites to escape recognition by the immune system. Xu and colleagues theorized that building an anti-flu antibody based on the IgM isotype -- an early, first-line immune responder known for its pentameric shape -- featuring more structural sites capable of binding to the HA protein as well as an IgG-level tight and precise target, would increase the antibody's neutralization capacity. The team constructed and evaluated 18 IgM antibodies based on existing IgG antibodies known to target different segments of the HA protein, landing on one IgM version that was superior in cell culture studies at neutralizing a panel of H1N1 and H3N2 flu viruses that have been circulating in people for at least 50 years. "The advantage is that we utilized the multivalency feature of IgM combined with the potency of a mature broadly neutralizing antibody," Xu said. "Multivalent means that this molecule has multiple arms to capture the virus, so the virus has very little chance to escape. "And because IgM is a large molecule, when it binds to the viral surface it effectively covers the surface, so it prevents receptor engagement." Experiments showed that even if a mutation affected this IgM antibody's strong attraction to a specific HA binding site, the antibody's large size and multivalency could help it outpace viral escape attempts. "By enhancing the capacity for it to recognize the mutated or differentiated viral surface regions, it can cross-protect against different influenza virus infections and can also work even after mutations occur," Xu said. In mouse experiments, researchers delivered a single dose of the IgM antibody in a nasal spray, intending to place a layer of the engineered antibody across the mucosal surfaces of the nasal cavities and lungs. "The primary purpose is to refuse viruses before they can get into a host cell," Xu said. "The mucosal immunity is more efficient than systemic immunity at intercepting, so the initial infection can be reduced or eliminated." Researchers followed the preventive nasal spray with lethal doses of H1N1 and H3N2 flu viruses. Compared to control animals that did not receive preventive treatment, mice receiving the IgM antibody had better outcomes: No mice got sick from the H3N2 strain, and most survived the H1N1 infection and recovered from symptoms. The molecules remained on the mouse mucosal surface for seven days, an indication that a single spray could last for weeks in humans. "The adhesiveness of the molecule extends its protection window, and that's one advantage of this strategy," Xu said. Though this work focused on historic and seasonal versions of influenza A, Xu said the team of structural biologists, immunologists and virologists see the findings as a proof of principle to help establish the IgM platform as a potential therapeutic technology for different viruses -- including bird flu, an influenza A H5 virus -- and even non-viral diseases like cancer. Xu co-led the study with Xinli Liu of the University of Houston, Tong-Ming Fu of IGM Biosciences Inc. and Zhiqiang An of the University of Texas Health Science Center at Houston. Additional co-authors from those institutions and the National Institute of Allergy and Infectious Diseases also worked on the study. This research was supported by grants from the National Institutes of Health, IGM Biosciences and the Welch Foundation. Influenza A, Metapneumovirus, and Influenza B dominate respiratory virus circulation in Delhi NCR - The News Mill

News Brief
Medical experts in Delhi NCR report a surge in respiratory viruses, with Influenza A (H3N2) as the primary cause, alongside Metapneumovirus, Coronaviruses, and Influenza B. Over 50% of children in outpatient departments present with symptoms like fever, cough, and body aches, though most cases are mild and self-limiting. The surge is linked to cooler temperatures, fluctuating humidity, and rising air pollution, which encourage indoor crowding. Health officials emphasize preventive measures such as hand hygiene, mask-wearing, and avoiding sick contacts. While hospitalizations remain stable, parents are urged to monitor children closely and seek medical advice for persistent or severe symptoms.
Original source text (English)
Medical experts in Delhi NCR have reported a widespread circulation of respiratory viruses, with Influenza A (H3N2) identified as the primary cause of the current surge. This wave features severe symptoms, including high-grade fevers and recovery periods often extending beyond 10 days. Discover more Medical Literature & Resources Health infection Dr Arjun Dang, CEO of Dr Dang’s Lab, named Influenza A (H3N2), Metapneumovirus, Coronaviruses, and Influenza B as the main viral agents present in the region. He associated the surge with cooler temperatures, fluctuating humidity, and increasing air pollution, conditions that encourage gatherings in crowded indoor spaces. Dr Dhiren Gupta, a paediatric specialist at Sir Ganga Ram Hospital, noted that more than 50% of children in outpatient departments present symptoms such as fever, cough, and body aches. Speaking to ANI, Dr Arjun Dang said, “The respiratory pathogen pattern shows predominant circulation of viral agents, particularly Influenza A (H3N2), Metapneumovirus, Coronaviruses, and Influenza B, which aligns with the changing weather in Delhi NCR marked by cooler temperatures, fluctuating humidity and rising air pollution leading to increased indoor crowding. The concurrent detection of multiple respiratory viruses suggests seasonal co-circulation/co-infections rather than isolated outbreaks, a pattern commonly observed during post-monsoon and winter transitions, while sporadic detection of Mycoplasma pneumoniae likely reflects background transmission or a secondary bacterial infection facilitated due to the immunocompromised state during viral infections.” Discover more respiratory virus Biological Sciences infections Dr Dhiren Gupta, Paediatric Intensive Care Specialist, Pulmonologist and Allergist, said, “There has been a noticeable rise in influenza cases, including H3N2 virus infection, in India over the past month. In our outpatient department, more than 50% of children are currently presenting with symptoms of influenza such as fever, cough, cold, sore throat, and body ache.” He added, “The good news is that most of these cases are mild and self-limiting. At present, there is no significant increase in hospitalisation, and the majority of children are recovering well with routine care.” He advised parents to monitor their children and seek medical advice promptly if fever persists. “Parents are advised not to panic. Adequate hydration, good nutrition, and proper rest are important for recovery. Early consultation with a doctor is recommended if a child has a high fever, breathing difficulty, poor oral intake, or symptoms lasting more than a few days,” he said. Dr Dhiren also stressed the importance of hygiene and preventive measures to limit virus transmission: “Simple preventive measures such as regular hand washing, wearing masks in crowded places, and avoiding close contact with sick individuals can help reduce the spread of infection.”

Influenza vaccine in UAE: When is best time to get shots ahead of back-to-school? - Khaleej Times

News Brief
As the back-to-school season approaches in the UAE, health experts are urging parents to prioritize the seasonal influenza vaccine for their children and themselves. Dr. Mansoor Anwar Habib, a consultant in Family Medicine and Occupational Health, emphasizes that September is the ideal time to get vaccinated, as it takes up to two weeks for the body to build immunity. While the vaccine does not completely prevent the flu, it significantly reduces symptom severity and transmission risk. The vaccine is suitable for all ages starting from six months and is strongly recommended for everyone, particularly high-risk groups. By vaccinating children, parents not only protect their families but also contribute to a healthier community, reducing missed school days and hospital visits during peak flu season.

SINCE 1978 Close the sidebar Life Sports Entertainment Food Travel Beauty & Health Fashion KT LUXE Dubai Dining Diaries Home Lifestyle Health Influenza vaccine in UAE: When is best time to get shots ahead of back-to-school? The seasonal flu vaccine is suitable for all ages, starting from six months old, and is strongly recommended for everyone in the community Published: Mon 27 Jul 2026, 1:49 PM By Ruqayya Al Qaydi Follow us Follow on Google Follow on WhatsApp Follow on Telegram As the back-to-school season approaches, UAE health experts are urging parents to prioritise the seasonal influenza vaccine alongside their back-to-school shopping, emphasising that early vaccination is crucial to protect children and the wider community from the flu virus. With students preparing to return to classrooms, doctors stress that September is the ideal time to get the flu shot, as it takes up to two weeks for the body to build immunity.Importance of early vaccination Dr Mansoor Anwar Habib, Consultant in Family Medicine and Occupational Health, highlighted the critical role of the seasonal flu vaccine in mitigating the severity of the illness. “The flu vaccine does not completely prevent the disease, but it significantly reduces the severity of the symptoms if a person is infected,” Dr Habib explained. Dr Mansoor Anwar Habib, Consultant in Family Medicine and Occupational Health By familiarising the immune system with the viral strains included in the annual vaccine, the body is better prepared to fight off the virus. “If the virus enters the body after vaccination, the immune system recognises it immediately and can handle it, resulting in much milder symptoms,” he noted. This reduction in symptom severity also plays a vital role in curbing the spread of the virus. “Because the vaccine lessens the symptoms, it consequently reduces the likelihood of transmitting the infection from one person to another,” Dr Habib added.Timing is everything Timing is a critical factor in the effectiveness of the flu vaccine. Dr Habib strongly advised taking the vaccine in early to mid-September, or as soon as it becomes available at health centres and hospitals. “It takes about two weeks for the vaccine to become effective. With the start of schools and the increased risk of spreading infections, the earlier one gets vaccinated, the better,” he stated. While availability may vary sometimes — arriving in early, mid, or late September depending on the healthcare facility — the overarching message is to act promptly. As parents prepare for the new academic year, buying uniforms and stationery is often on the top of ones mind. However, Dr Habib urged families to add the flu vaccine to their checklist. “Just as parents remember to buy school supplies for their children, they should also remember to vaccinate their children and themselves,” he emphasised. The seasonal flu vaccine is suitable for all ages, starting from six months old, and is strongly recommended for everyone in the community. Dr Habib further noted that the vaccine will be available at health centres and hospitals from the beginning of September onwards.Broader community protection The call for early vaccination aligns with broader health guidelines in the UAE. The Ministry of Health and Prevention (MoHap) consistently advocates for annual flu vaccinations, particularly for high-risk groups, including young children, the elderly, pregnant women, and individuals with chronic medical conditions. Recent studies have underscored the impact of pediatric flu vaccines. Research from Harvard Medical School found that vaccinating children aged two to five can reduce infection rates by up to 14 per cent, highlighting children as key transmitters of the virus due to their time spent in close-contact environments like schools and nurseries. By ensuring children are vaccinated, parents not only protect their own families but also contribute to a healthier community, reducing missed school days and hospital visits during the peak flu season. Dr Habib call to action for all residents: “We recommend everyone in the community get vaccinated. The vaccine will be available at health centres and hospitals from the beginning of September onwards.” ALSO READ: Back-to-school bugs: UAE doctors warn parents not to ignore these signs ‘1 missed vaccine can be life-threatening’: UAE doctors back Dh20,000 fine plan Second peak of flu season in UAE? Doctors say never too late to get vaccinated Ruqayya Al Qaydi Ruqayya Alqaydi is a Special Correspondent with an endless curiosity to learn. Like a journalist on... More ralqaydi@khaleejtimes.com

科学家绘制出甲型流感如何劫持人体细胞的图谱

Scientists Map How Influenza A Hijacks Human Cells - indica News
新闻简报科学家已绘制出首张详细图谱,展示了甲型流感病毒蛋白在感染过程中与人体细胞蛋白的相互作用,该研究发表在《Nature Microbiology》上。这项研究由汉堡欧洲分子生物学实验室(EMBL Hamburg)及合作机构的研究人员进行,他们采用了一种特殊的交联质谱技术结合AlphaFold建模,揭示了病毒劫持人体细胞的两种主要机制。研究表明,甲型流感病毒会破坏细胞核中的核旁斑(paraspeckles),释放RNA结合蛋白以支持其复制。这一突破可能导致更好的流感药物和疫苗,并可能适用于其他具有大流行潜力的病毒,包括H5N1禽流感。季节性流感目前每年在全球导致高达650,000人死亡。

事件
科学家们在同行评审期刊《自然微生物学》上发表了第一份关于甲型流感病毒蛋白与完整感染细胞内人类细胞蛋白之间直接相互作用的详细图谱。这项研究得到证实,代表着理解甲型流感如何劫持人类细胞的突破。
规模、影响与风险
Seasonal influenza causes up to 650,000 deaths worldwide annually and leads to severe illness in an estimated 3-5 million people. Influenza A has also caused several pandemics, including the 1918 Spanish Flu. The study does not report current outbreak cases or deaths.
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究涉及使用适用于病毒感染细胞的交联质谱 (XL-MS) 并结合 AlphaFold 建模来开发定制工作流程。该研究揭示了甲型流感劫持人类细胞的两种主要方式,并表明破坏paraspeckles可能会削弱细胞防御机制。该方法可应用于其他具有大流行潜力的病毒,包括 H5N1 禽流感。该研究已完成并发表,但尚未报告正在进行的调查。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Scientists Map How Influenza A Hijacks Human Cells - indica News

News Brief
Scientists have created the first detailed map showing how influenza A virus proteins interact with human cell proteins during infection, published in Nature Microbiology. The study, conducted by researchers at EMBL Hamburg and collaborating institutions, used a specialized cross-linking mass spectrometry technique combined with AlphaFold modeling to reveal two major mechanisms by which the virus hijacks human cells. The research shows that influenza A disrupts paraspeckles in the cell nucleus, releasing RNA-binding proteins to support its replication. This breakthrough could lead to better flu drugs and vaccines and may be applicable to other viruses with pandemic potential, including H5N1 bird flu. Seasonal influenza currently causes up to 650,000 deaths worldwide annually.
Original source text (English)
Share the news iNDICA NEWS BUREAU- Scientists have created the first detailed map showing how the influenza A virus takes control of human cells during infection, a breakthrough that could help researchers develop better flu drugs and vaccines. The study, published in the peer-reviewed journal Nature Microbiology, was led by researchers at EMBL Hamburg in collaboration with scientists from the Leibniz Research Institute for Molecular Pharmacology (FMP), Charite – Universitatsmedizin Berlin, and other research partners. It provides the first large-scale structural map of direct interactions between influenza A virus proteins and human cell proteins inside intact infected cells. Seasonal influenza causes up to 650,000 deaths worldwide every year and leads to severe illness in an estimated 3 million to 5 million people. Influenza A has also caused several pandemics, including the 1918 Spanish Flu. To carry out the research, scientists developed a customized workflow that allowed them to observe how viral and human proteins interact directly inside infected cells. They used a specialized version of cross-linking mass spectrometry (XL-MS), adapted specifically for virus-infected cells. The technique enabled them to capture short-lived and location-specific protein interactions that earlier methods often failed to detect. The researchers then combined the XL-MS data with a modified version of AlphaFold, the artificial intelligence-based protein structure prediction tool. This helped them build detailed models showing how viral and human proteins physically interact inside infected cells. The study revealed two major ways influenza A hijacks human cells. One involved the virus’s surface protein, hemagglutinin, which relies on host proteins as it moves through the cell’s transport and processing system. These host proteins help fold and modify the viral protein, allowing the virus to continue its infection process. The researchers also discovered that influenza A causes paraspeckles, small droplet-like structures inside the cell nucleus, to break apart during infection. This releases RNA-binding proteins that the virus can use to support its own replication. The team found this effect consistently across different human cell lines and influenza strains, suggesting it is part of the virus’s infection strategy rather than a random side effect. Scientists believe disrupting paraspeckles may also weaken the cell’s natural defense mechanisms, making it easier for the virus to multiply. The research brought together scientific expertise and facilities from several institutions. Cross-linking mass spectrometry experiments were carried out at Charite in Berlin, glycoproteomics analyses were conducted at the EMBL Proteomics Core Facility, AlphaFold modeling was performed on the EMBL Compute Cluster, and microscopy imaging was completed at the Centre for Structural Systems Biology’s Advanced Light and Fluorescence Microscopy Facility. The researchers said the approach could be used to study many other viruses, not just influenza. They believe the combination of in-cell cross-linking, structural modeling, and cell biology can help scientists understand how different viruses interact with human cells during infection. The team also said the method could be applied to viruses with pandemic potential, including H5N1 bird flu, to identify the interaction networks that help them multiply inside human cells and uncover new targets for future antiviral treatments. Photo: Dmytro Vynohradov/ Unsplash

Event
Scientists published the first detailed map of direct interactions between influenza A virus proteins and human cell proteins inside intact infected cells in the peer-reviewed journal Nature Microbiology. The study is confirmed and represents a breakthrough in understanding how influenza A hijacks human cells.
Scale, impact and risk
Seasonal influenza causes up to 650,000 deaths worldwide annually and leads to severe illness in an estimated 3-5 million people. Influenza A has also caused several pandemics, including the 1918 Spanish Flu. The study does not report current outbreak cases or deaths.
Response, status and uncertainty
The research involved developing a customized workflow using cross-linking mass spectrometry (XL-MS) adapted for virus-infected cells, combined with AlphaFold modeling. The study revealed two major ways influenza A hijacks human cells and suggested that disrupting paraspeckles may weaken cell defense mechanisms. The approach could be applied to other viruses with pandemic potential, including H5N1 bird flu. The research is complete and published, with no ongoing investigation reported.

Most Americans Unaware of CDC's Pregnancy Vaccine Recommendations - Drug Topics
新闻简报一项新的调查显示,美国人对怀孕期间 CDC 推荐的 疫苗 普遍存在不确定性,对关键 疫苗 的认知度在 23% 到 48% 之间。安纳伯格公共政策中心于 2026 年 7 月 1 日至 5 日对 1,031 名美国成年人进行的研究发现,“不确定”是所有推荐的 疫苗 中最常见的反应,包括季节性流感、Tdap、RSV 和乙型肝炎。育龄妇女的认知度稍高,但仍然存在很大的不确定性。研究结果强调了药剂师作为顾问的关键作用,特别是在不断变化的联邦指导和专业协会建议并不总是一致的情况下。该调查强调了政策变化期间公共卫生沟通面临的挑战。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Most Americans Unaware of CDC's Pregnancy Vaccine Recommendations - Drug Topics

News Brief
A new survey reveals widespread uncertainty among Americans about CDC-recommended vaccines during pregnancy, with awareness ranging from 23% to 48% for key vaccines. Conducted July 1-5, 2026, among 1,031 US adults, the Annenberg Public Policy Center study found that 'not sure' was the most common response across all recommended vaccines, including seasonal flu, Tdap, RSV, and hepatitis B. Women of childbearing age showed slightly higher awareness but still significant uncertainty. The findings underscore the critical role of pharmacists as counselors, particularly amid evolving federal guidance and professional society recommendations that are not always aligned. The survey highlights challenges in public health communication during a period of policy flux.
Original source text (English)
Most Americans are unaware or unsure which vaccines the CDC recommends during pregnancy, according to a nationally representative survey of US adults released July 22, 2026, by the Annenberg Public Policy Center (APPC) of the University of Pennsylvania. 1 A new survey shows widespread uncertainty about which vaccines the CDC recommends during pregnancy, underscoring the counseling role pharmacists play. | Image Credit: Marina Demidiuk - stock.adobe.com The survey, fielded July 1 to 5, 2026, among 1031 US adults, found that even the best-known recommendation—the seasonal flu vaccine—was correctly identified by less than half of respondents. 1 The findings raise questions for pharmacists, who administer a substantial share of vaccinations and are often the last clinical touchpoint before a pregnant patient decides whether to vaccinate. Awareness of Recommended Vaccines Is Low According to CDC guidance, 4 vaccines are routinely recommended during pregnancy: the seasonal influenza vaccine; the Tdap vaccine (tetanus, diphtheria, and pertussis), preferably between 27 and 36 weeks' gestation; the respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) vaccine, given during weeks 32 to 36 in RSV season; and the hepatitis B vaccine for those not already vaccinated. Among all adults surveyed, awareness of these recommendations ranged from 23% for the hepatitis B vaccine to 48% for the seasonal flu vaccine. 1 For the COVID-19 vaccine, more than half of respondents did not know whether the CDC recommends it. The agency's current guidance frames COVID-19 vaccination during pregnancy as a matter of individual decision-making rather than a routine recommendation, a position the American College of Obstetricians and Gynecologists (ACOG) has said does not go far enough, as ACOG "continues to recommend that all pregnant and lactating individuals receive an updated COVID-19 vaccine." 1 Women aged 18 to 49 years were significantly more knowledgeable than other adults on most items, correctly identifying the hepatitis B vaccine recommendation 30% of the time and the flu vaccine recommendation 60% of the time, compared with 21% and 43%, respectively, among other adults. Awareness of the RSV vaccine recommendation did not differ significantly between the 2 groups. 2 Uncertainty Dominates the Findings Across every vaccine the CDC recommends during pregnancy, plus the COVID-19 vaccine, "not sure" was the most common response. Between 45% and 66% of all adults said they were not sure whether the CDC recommends the flu, Tdap, RSV, hepatitis B, or COVID-19 vaccines during pregnancy. Among women of childbearing age, that uncertainty ranged from 32% to 58%. 1 "Our findings suggest that many Americans do not know which vaccines are recommended during pregnancy," Ken Winneg, PhD, APPC's managing director of survey research, said in the news release. 1 "The encouraging news is that relatively few people think that the CDC recommends vaccines that are not advised during pregnancy." Relatively small shares of respondents incorrectly believed the CDC recommends vaccines that are not advised during pregnancy: 21% for the measles, mumps, and rubella (MMR) vaccine, 17% for the human papillomavirus (HPV) vaccine, and 17% for the varicella (chickenpox) vaccine. 1 Women of childbearing age were no more likely than other adults to hold these incorrect beliefs. For HPV, for example, 20% of women aged 18 to 49 years incorrectly said the vaccine is recommended during pregnancy, compared with 16% of other adults, a difference the release characterized as not statistically significant. 1 What This Means for Pharmacists "Many Americans routinely get their immunizations from their primary health care providers or at local pharmacies," Patrick E. Jamieson, director of APPC's Annenberg Health and Risk Communication Institute, said. 1 "It is critical that those professionals pay special attention to vaccine recommendations for recipients who are pregnant." That counseling responsibility comes at a moment of unusual flux in federal vaccine policy. CDC published an updated ACIP charter this year shifting governance authority toward the CDC director and removing fixed meeting-schedule requirements, a change that the Infectious Diseases Society of America and several cosigning organizations warned could "lead to confusion and delays that threaten public access to lifesaving vaccines and evidence-based, accurate information." 3 Separately, ACOG withdrew as an ACIP liaison organization in February 2026 and released its own 2026 Maternal Immunization Schedule in June, endorsed by 13 organizations, including the American Pharmacists Association, covering the same 4 vaccine categories tracked in the APPC survey plus COVID-19. 4 With federal guidance and professional-society recommendations not always aligned, pharmacists may need to reconcile more than one source of guidance when counseling pregnant patients at the counter. Survey Methodology The survey was conducted for APPC by SSRS, an independent research company, using a nationally representative probability sample from SSRS's Opinion Panel. 5 Data collection ran from July 1 to July 5, 2026, via web (n = 1001) and telephone (n = 30), among 1031 U.S. adults, with a margin of sampling error of ±3.4 percentage points at the 95% confidence level and a design effect of 1.23. The survey completion rate was 53.5%, with a weighted response rate of 56.2%. 5 REFERENCES 1. Rozansky M. Most Americans are unsure which vaccines the CDC recommends during pregnancy. News release. Swine Flu Cases Surge: Common Symptoms Of H1N1 Virus To Look Out For And When To Seek Medical Help - Republic World

News Brief
In July 2026, Nagpur reported the first death from Influenza A (H1N1) this year, with 25 cases recorded since January. The outbreak is described as seasonal, not pandemic-related. Symptoms include fever, cough, sore throat, and body aches, typically appearing 1-4 days post-exposure. While most recover within a week, severe cases may require medical attention, particularly for high-risk groups like young children, elderly, pregnant women, and those with chronic conditions. The virus spreads through respiratory droplets, emphasizing the importance of early symptom recognition and timely medical consultation.
Original source text (English)
Amid the rising cases of swine flu reported in the month of July, Nagpur has reported the first death from Influenza A (H1N1) this year. As per reports, nearly 25 cases of the H1N1 virus have been reported in Nagpur since the beginning of the year. However, the swine flu is considered a seasonal viral infection now and not a pandemic, thus eliminating the cause of worry and panic. Most people affected by the virus recover within a week of being infected, while some develop severe complications. The H1N1 virus is a contagious flu that is transmitted through infected people. It mainly spreads through respiratory droplets released when an infected person coughs, sneezes or talks. While the symptoms can mimic those of a common cold and cough, it is important to recognise the correct symptoms early to know when to consult a doctor. The symptoms of H1N1 are similar to those of seasonal influenza and usually appear one to four days after exposure to the virus. Some of the most common signs, as per Mayo Clinic, include: High fever or chills Persistent cough Sore throat Runny or blocked nose Headache Body and muscle aches Extreme tiredness or fatigue Sneezing Vomiting and diarrhoea, particularly in children, though adults may experience them as well. Although mild cases can often be managed at home with adequate rest, hydration and medications prescribed by a healthcare provider, certain warning signs require immediate medical attention. Seek medical care if you experience: Difficulty breathing or shortness of breath Persistent chest pain or pressure Bluish lips or fingertips High fever that does not improve or returns after subsiding Severe dizziness, confusion or difficulty staying awake Persistent vomiting leading to dehydration Symptoms that worsen instead of improving after a few days People at higher risk of severe illness include young children, adults aged 65 years and above, pregnant women, individuals with weakened immunity, and those with chronic conditions such as diabetes, asthma, heart disease or kidney disorders. Nat Microbiol:流感病毒如何“劫持”人细胞?科学家绘制出一张分子级别的入侵地图 - 生物谷

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报科学家们绘制了甲型流感病毒如何劫持人类细胞的分子水平图,发表在《自然微生物学》上。利用先进的细胞内交联质谱和改进的 AlphaFold 预测,研究人员以近原子分辨率绘制了病毒和 宿主 蛋白之间的直接接触位点。该研究揭示了两种关键的病毒劫持途径:一种是在细胞质膜运输系统中,另一种是针对称为副斑点的核无膜细胞器。该研究确定了以前未知的病毒-宿主 相互作用位点,并为 抗病毒药物 药物提供了精确的靶标,这些药物可以破坏病毒蛋白的成熟或防止副斑斑的分解。这种“细胞劫持地图”为下一代针对流感和其他潜在大流行病毒的 抗病毒药物 策略奠定了基础。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-07-24。证据中未明确报告事件日期。
地点与对象
证据中没有具体说明。由欧洲分子生物学实验室和其他机构的科学家进行的研究。
事件
已证实的研究结果:科学家以近原子分辨率在完整的受感染细胞中绘制了甲型流感病毒与人类 宿主 蛋白之间的直接接触位点,揭示了两种病毒劫持途径。
规模、影响与风险
这是一项研究,而不是 暴发 报告。该研究确定了甲型流感病毒感染的潜在药物靶点。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究完成并发表在《自然微生物学》上。该研究使用了优化的细胞内交联质谱法和改进的 AlphaFold 结构预测。研究结果以确认结果的形式呈现,没有提及未解决的信息。

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
Nat Microbiol:流感病毒如何“劫持”人细胞?科学家绘制出一张分子级别的入侵地图 - 生物谷
News Brief
Scientists have created a molecular-level map of how influenza A virus hijacks human cells, published in Nature Microbiology. Using advanced in-cell cross-linking mass spectrometry and improved AlphaFold predictions, researchers mapped direct contact sites between viral and host proteins at near-atomic resolution. The study reveals two key viral hijacking pathways: one in the cytoplasmic membrane transport system and another targeting nuclear membraneless organelles called paraspeckles. The research identifies previously unknown virus-host interaction sites and provides precise targets for antiviral drugs that could disrupt viral protein maturation or prevent the disassembly of paraspeckles. This 'cellular hijacking map' offers a foundation for next-generation antiviral strategies against influenza and potentially other pandemic viruses.
Original source text (Chinese)
资讯 生物在线 品牌会议 行云公开课 登录 注册 生物谷APP 搜索 最新会议 空中讲坛 热点推荐 生物在线 资讯 生物在线 品牌会议 行云公开课 活动集锦 ePoch专访 企业库 疾病库 投资库 期刊学会 热点事件 园区报道 打开APP 首页 生物研究 详情页 Nat Microbiol:流感病毒如何“劫持”人细胞?科学家绘制出一张分子级别的入侵地图 血凝素 甲型流感病毒 无膜细胞器 细胞内交联 来源:生物谷原创 2026-07-24 11:00 科学家们通过研究首次在完整感染细胞内,以近原子尺度的结构分辨率,绘制了甲型流感病毒与人类宿主蛋白之间的直接接触图谱。 每年全球有高达65万人死于季节性流感,另有300万至500万人因此患上严重疾病。大流行毒株的威胁更为严峻—1918年西班牙大流感、2009年甲型H1N1大流行,都曾在短时间内席卷全球。流感病毒之所以难缠,不仅在于其表面抗原频繁变异,更在于它侵入宿主细胞后,能够熟练地“策反”细胞的分子机器,将其变为病毒复制的工厂。然而,病毒与宿主蛋白之间究竟在何处、以何种方式直接“交手”,此前一直缺乏系统性的全景视图。 日前,一篇发表在国际杂志Nature Microbiology上题为“Mapping in-cell protein contact sites reveals hijacking of paraspeckles during influenza A virus infection”的研究报告中,来自欧洲分子生物学实验室等机构的科学家们通过研究首次在完整感染细胞内,以近原子尺度的结构分辨率,绘制了甲型流感病毒与人类宿主蛋白之间的直接接触图谱。这项研究不仅发现了多个此前未知的病毒-宿主相互作用位点,更揭示了两条清晰的病毒操控路径:一条发生在细胞质的膜性运输系统中,另一条则直击细胞核内的无膜细胞器。 文章中,研究人员采用的核心技术是经过特殊优化的细胞内交联质谱联用技术。传统方法需要先破碎细胞再检测蛋白互作,这极易产生“假阳性”——原本在完整细胞中永不相见的蛋白,可能在试管中意外相遇;而脆弱或依赖于特定空间位置的接触,又极易丢失。新型交联质谱技术则能在细胞保持完整的状态下,将空间上邻近的蛋白“锁定”并加以识别。配合改良版的AlphaFold结构预测算法,实验数据被直接输入模型,指导预测病毒与宿主蛋白复合体的三维构型。这种“实验+计算”的闭环策略,大大提升了预测可靠性,尤其对于进化上高度动态的病毒-宿主互作界面,效果尤为显著。 甲型流感病毒感染过程中的细胞内交联 该研究的第一条重要发现,围绕病毒表面关键蛋白—血凝素,血凝素负责识别并入侵宿主细胞,但其本身必须经过宿主内质网-高尔基体膜性系统的精确修饰与折叠,才能具备感染活性。借助交联质谱,研究人员系统追踪了血凝素在分泌通路中的“旅行轨迹”,并鉴定了与之发生直接接触的多个宿主蛋白。其中部分宿主因子此前从未被关联到病毒感染过程。这些蛋白像流水线上的质检员与装配工,协助病毒蛋白完成特定糖基化修饰,使其成熟为具备功能的构象。这一结果精准定位了病毒“借用”宿主蛋白加工体系的节点,也为设计干扰血凝素成熟的抗病毒药物提供了明确靶点。 更令人意外的发现来自细胞核内,研究人员观察到,感染甲型流感病毒后,核内一种名为“副斑点”的无膜细胞器发生了系统性、不可逆的解体。副斑点属于相分离凝聚体,内部富集了多种RNA结合蛋白和长链非编码RNA,被认为参与基因调控和应激反应。在多个细胞系、多种流感毒株的测试中,这一解体现象均稳定出现,提示这并非感染的副产物,而更像一种病毒精心策划的策略。进一步的机制解析表明,病毒至少动用了三把“钥匙”来打开副斑点:病毒核蛋白和非结构蛋白1直接与副斑点核心组分发生物理接触;病毒内切酶PA-X则负责降解副斑点内关键的长链非编码RNA;同时,病毒聚合酶对宿主RNA聚合酶II的抑制,进一步加剧了解体进程。副斑点瓦解后,原本被束缚其中的RNA结合蛋白被释放,这些游离的宿主因子随即被病毒招募,转而促进病毒自身的转录与复制。换言之,病毒不仅拆掉了细胞核内的“应激堡垒”,还顺手将里面的“守军”收缴改编为己用。 研究人员强调,这套“细胞内交联-结构建模-功能验证”的组合拳,完全可应用于其他具有大流行潜力的病毒,例如高致病性禽流感H5N1。在真实感染场景下原位捕捉分子接触,不仅能让科学家看清病毒如何“动手”,更能揭示哪些蛋白间的握手指尖最脆弱、最易被药物介入打断。对于长期困扰人类的流感病毒而言,一张精细的分子劫持地图,或许正是通往下一代抗病毒策略的必经之路。(生物谷Bioon.com) 参考文献: Kotova, I., Mühlberg, L., Gilep, K. et al. Mapping in-cell protein contact sites reveals hijacking of paraspeckles during influenza A virus infection. News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报发表在《欧洲药物化学杂志》上的一项新研究确定了两种新型香豆素杂合体 LMed 93 和 LMed 99,它们是甲型流感病毒 (IAV) RNA 依赖性 RNA 聚合酶的有效抑制剂。通过计算筛选和体外验证,这些化合物对 H1N1 和 H5N1 病毒株表现出强大的活性,可减少病毒 RNA 和蛋白质表达。研究表明,这些杂交体靶向病毒聚合酶复合物的 PA-PB1 界面,为开发广谱抗流感药物提供了有希望的先导。虽然该研究为 抗病毒药物 的发现提供了强大的框架,但仍需要进一步的研究来确认直接分子靶标并评估体内功效。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于2026-07-22;在此日期之前进行的研究。
地点与对象
证据中未注明;使用 MDCK II 细胞和 IAV H1N1 (A/WSN/1933) 和 H5N1 (A/Thailand/KAN-1/2004) 病毒株进行体外研究。
事件
发表在《欧洲药物化学杂志》上的研究描述了针对甲型流感病毒 RNA 依赖性 RNA 聚合酶的新型香豆素杂合体(LMed 93 和 LMed 99),并通过计算和体外研究得到证实。
规模、影响与风险
体外研究;没有人类病例或 暴发 的报告。化合物对 H1N1 和 H5N1 病毒具有有效抑制作用,对 H5N1 病毒的 EC50 值为 4.9 μM (LMed 99) 和 9.13 μM (LMed 93)。没有提供直接的风险评估。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究属于临床前研究;没有公共卫生反应或调查的报告。尽管有计算和实验证据,但研究的局限性包括依赖体外模型和未解决的分子靶标。
